Tier B (`Company`) is founder-owned, versioned in the store, edited live, the org chart included (`roles:`, `units:`), whose every seat handle and unit key a write mints into the stored document (`MintIdentities`), so correcting a name in the STORED document never moves an identity and a different handle is a different seat, a FILE is minted afresh from its names on every import, which is why the shipped examples declare every handle and key. The Dashboard Assistant's block (`assistant:`, `assistant.go`) is off unless enabled, and its chain is admitted only when every member is an entry whose harness's local AND web tools are denied; it is an ADMISSION rule, so a stored revision that breaks it still applies, and `Company.AssistantChain` is the one place the chain is handed out, refusing it there with the same problems, so such a revision leaves the assistant unavailable and every seat running. A third channel beside errors: `Warnings()` on each tier is what is VALID and worth knowing before applying, a declined fsync's window, a trim that will never advance, a human seat nobody can be messaged at on the Mattermost or Slack the company talks on, a company that declares NO human seat and so can admit nobody (every person holds one, ADR-0032, right for a company its agents run alone through Tier A tokens, so a warning and never a rule), a seat ceiling the company's leaves idle, printed by `crewlet validate`, and LOGGED where every deployment sees them, because most never run `validate` (an image, a chart or an operator renders the document and starts the node): Tier A's by `crewlet run` once per start, a `config_advisory` line each, judged on the document with the run's flags FOLDED IN (`overrideNode`, `overrideLogging`) and held to every Tier A rule again before a line is written (`validateFolded`, whose refusal names the flags), so a `-debug` run is not told to pass `-debug` and no advisory precedes a refusal a flag drew, and a company's by every node once per epoch it APPLIES (the same event, `config=company`, on the reconcile's success branch beside the dangling-reference and admission lines), which is the only report a company seeded from a file ever gets, never affecting an exit code, because a warning that can fail a build is one somebody suppresses. Tier A is the root of trust and therefore resolves with `EnvOnly`, it holds the keys to the secret store, so it can never read a value out of it, and resolves BEFORE it decodes, TYPED by the field each reference lands in: into text the value is that text, character for character (a resolved `null` is not an absence), and into a number or a switch it must be the WHOLE value and is read, trimmed, as the literal it spells, because substituting text everywhere left no number field able to take a reference while the docs promised one anywhere. A reference spliced into a number, or one resolving to nothing there, is refused by name rather than guessed at, and so is a fraction bound for a whole number, which the decoder used to truncate without a word (`api.port: 8080.9` ran as 8080), and — in BOTH tiers, written or resolved — a number that is not finite (`.nan`, `.inf`), which the decoder reads faithfully and NaN then passes every bound a validator compares it with (`refuseNonFinite`). `coordination.lease_ttl_seconds` has a FLOOR (`MinLeaseTTL`, 15 s, the reasons at the constant), because the seat lease TTL is also the watchdog's exit threshold and nothing bounded it from below, so a TTL of a few seconds let a busy node end itself; 0 still takes the seat layer's 45, which an engine test holds above the floor since config cannot import it. Tier B's secrets are `${VAR}` POINTERS, stored verbatim, resolved only where a provider or transport is constructed, so a Tier B reference is judged as the text it is, and a field whose consumer resolves only a WHOLE one (the Mattermost and Slack bot tokens, the Mattermost username) refuses one spliced into other text rather than sending the braces to the vendor. The schema admits what the DECODER reads rather than what a Go type spells, and a test offers every position of both tiers every such value. It also marks every credential position `x-crewlet-secret: true`, read through the same predicate (`secrets.Field`) that redaction masks by, and a test runs the redactor over a fully populated document and holds the marked positions to the masked ones in both directions. THE KEYRING IS REQUIRED ON EVERY NODE, serving or not, a node without `data` included (`secrets.keys`, `validateTopology`): every state-log record a data node applies is signed and verified under it, every request a node without data sends a data node's estate service is signed under it (ADR-0031), the company document a node fetches from its peers is authenticated by its seal, and a node serving the API signs every session cookie with it, so `Secrets.Cipher` refuses an empty keyring rather than handing back the nil that used to mean plaintext. AND SO IS A STREAM THAT SURVIVES A RESTART on every broker MEMBER (`stream.store_dir` on an embedded member, a single node included, `Stream.Durable`, asked by `Validate` and again at `engine.New`'s door before anything opens), while a leaf, a node without `data`, which keeps no stream, is REFUSED one: every data node keeps its state logs on the stream from boot, company or none, so an in-memory member is a node whose first restart leaves it unable to serve. It was a CROSS-TIER rule (`CheckTiers`, deleted with it) asked only of a company, which let through exactly the node started with none, and no rule needs both documents any more. THREE MORE SETTINGS STOP BEING OPTIONAL the moment a node SERVES THE API (`Bootstrap.ServesAPI`: `api.port` non-zero AND the `ingress` role, on the Bootstrap rather than the API block because half the answer is the node block's roles, and a probe-only port needs none of them), and `api.go` is where the rule lives: `api.external_url` (the cookie's Secure flag and __Host- prefix, the origin a write and a socket handshake are judged against, and every link a person follows, the engine is behind a TLS-terminating proxy and can read none of it off the request; it is the vendors' address too unless `api.public` opens a listener for them, whose own `api.public.external_url` is then REQUIRED and is what `API.WebhookBase` answers, so Tier B carries no address at all), `api.auth.admin` (`iam.AdminPosture`: whether THIS node serves `admin`, `serve`, or `refuse`, which clamps admin away and keeps every app, the apps expanded before the clamp, applied per node per request so a mixed fleet is a LEGAL state each node publishes its posture for; the zero is refused naming the field, and an unknown name in a Tier A token's `grants` is refused naming the three, as is `admin` on a `refuse` node) and at least one `api.auth.tokens` entry (a fresh estate is empty, so its session is what creates the company and invites the first person onto a human seat it declares, and the way back in when an administrator has locked themselves out). A token's value is checked on what the `${VAR}` RESOLVES to, which is the only place the floor bites, and `api.trusted_proxies` is a CIDR list and never a bool, because the question is whether THIS peer is the proxy. `api.auth.company_writers` names the Tier A tokens that alone may change a MANAGED company document (ADR-0030), each entry held to the token-id grammar and judged on the CREDENTIAL a write came through (`MayWriteCompany`, which the viewer answer reads too), so a person's session, a machine token and a lead's subtree write are all refused while it is set. `allow_anonymous_read` and `api.auth.disabled` are DELETED, and a file still carrying either is refused at its line like any unknown key (`KnownFields`, so no retired posture can be silently inverted when the key itself is refused) but BY NAME, through `removedKeys` (`removed.go`), which says the key was removed and what replaces it, because the generic refusal tells somebody upgrading an old file to check a spelling no spelling passes, keyed on the block's Go type, since `disabled:` anywhere but `api.auth` is an ordinary typo and reads like one: the first was a bool whose safe value was its zero and which was `omitempty`, so closing it did not survive an export round trip, and the second authenticated the EMPTY credential into full operator authority, and so is `api.auth.oidc`, because nobody signs in through a third party any more, and with it went the BACKEND SELECTOR: password sign-in is always served, since a deployment nobody was invited to already signs nobody in, and `api.auth.totp` (UNSET IS `required`, so `optional` is always somebody's explicit choice), `accept_insecure` and `min_password_length` sit directly under `api.auth`; and so is `api.auth.bootstrap`, because the Tier A token IS the credential a company has before it has anybody, so the first person is invited under it like everybody after them, onto a human seat. `DiffOrg` is what a `/config` write CHANGES in Tier B, object by object: every seat (by handle) and unit (by key) added, removed, moved or edited, each with the PLACES it reaches, where it was and is, a unit itself when its own fields change, what a NEW `lead:` or `manages:` entry names, every object whose `lead:` or `manages:` entry already states the id an ADDED seat or unit takes (a dangling `lead: ghost` is taken over by adding `ghost` anywhere), plus the KEYS another system finds a seat or unit by that it sets, clears or alters (a project, space, channel, email or contact identity, all `admin`'s: a project outlives the unit that declared it and an account exists at its vendor, so who else holds a key is not a fact two documents state, and judged against the document's own objects a lead declared an orphaned project their team's and became its lead), the credentials it touches, each credential field compared WHOLE, keys included (an `mcp_env` key with nothing under it starts that per-seat server for the seat, and a walk of the strings alone found nothing in it), with every `${VAR}` it touches in ANY field counted among them (a seat's `email` and contact ids resolve a whole reference through the secret store and the engine's own environment, and are recited to whoever looks the seat up), and the settings keys that differ, because the org chart's leads may write their own subtree and each of those places is a subtree decision (internal/authz's). Only what the write changes is judged, and two objects on one id are placed at the root, nobody's subtree. THE EXECUTOR RULES ARE THE WHOLE DOCUMENT'S (`company.go`, `budget.go`), each RUNNABLE because no turn of the seat it names could run: a company with an agent seat and a `providers.llm` entry needs `providers.sandbox`, one refusal at the block naming the first such seat (`validateSandboxPlacement`; a company with no model yet is an org chart written before its credentials and needs none), and every runner-capable member of every agent seat's executor chain has to resolve a cell the catalogue configures; a seat whose executor chain holds no `claude-code` or `opencode` member is refused at its `llm`, or once at the `agent` of the entry seats with none fall to (`validateExecutors`), while a chain that mixes one with another harness is a warning (`executorWarnings`), since the other harness still serves the seat's reviewer, workers and side passes; `run_in` on a harness with no in-box runner is refused as a placement nothing reads (`LLMProvider.validate`); and a `token_budget` ceiling below `turn_engine.executor_min_budget_tokens` is refused at the ceiling (`validateExecutorFloor`), because such a seat parks as starved before its first launch and stays parked; a `providers.llm` key matches `ProviderKeyPattern` (a DNS label with dots and underscores) and no two keys share a credential bundle variable, refused on a write and warned on apply, since two such keys silently shared one login; and `ProviderReferences` is the ONE walk of every field that names an entry (a seat's `llm` as a scalar, list or mapping, its flat per-phase fields, `assistant.llm` and `assistant.deep_work`), each with the segments of the written field, which `validateProviderKeys` reads too; `MCPServerReferences` is the same for an MCP server (every seat's and unit's `mcp_env` block keyed on it and every `sandbox.mcp.servers` entry, a block the engine reads by its own name marked `EngineRead`), which the admission rule refusing a field that names no declared server (`validateMCPServerReferences`) and a server's removal (`RemoveMCPServer`) both read |
| `configplane` | The control plane: how a fleet agrees which company configuration it is running, and what a node does when it does not have the current one. An append-only ACTIVATION POINTER names the epoch and every node writes its own apply status; a node reads both and picks a posture. Append-only because re-activating an UNCHANGED revision is the credential-rotation gesture, and a pointer keyed on revision id would rebuild nothing on exactly that operation. THE POINTER IS THE COMPANY (ADR-0057, in the package doc's "Three facts about a company's configuration"): every config read and the base of every config write answer it whenever there is one, on every node alike, never a node's own row, and a node keeps three facts of its own about it, each with one writer — what it BOOTS on, moved online only to a revision its engine has applied; the FLEET's revision as it last saw it, refused or not, which is what it restores when the pointer is gone; and an offline OFFER, published only by a compare-and-set over the base it was built on. One flag for all three made a node boot on a revision it had refused, walked a write back, and published an offline change over a fleet that had moved on. `RevisionOf` is the ONE mapping from the pointer and the body it carries to a history row, for the reconciler, the config API and the boot alike, at the pointer's instant and under the origin's record, because each reader built its own and they disagreed on whose record a revision was — and it is why this package imports `coord` and `store` |
| `seat` | Which seats this node runs. The placement policy is deliberately dumb — greedy claim to `ceil(seats / live nodes)`, converging in BOTH directions, `preferred` orders the attempt and never gates it; a change to the seat set — an apply adding or removing a role — asks for a pass at once (`Resweep`) rather than leaving every new seat unclaimed until the tick, and so does the CLAIM GATE OPENING (`Config.Ready`), which the host cannot see and the engine asks for — and an asked pass the gate withheld, which claimed and gave back nothing, spends nothing of the one early pass an interval honours, or the ask that says the gate has opened waited for the tick behind an apply's that found the node still catching up. `Host.Start`'s first pass is the only synchronous claim, and on a boot behind its logs it claims nothing; it runs with the HEARTBEAT ALREADY TURNING, because the pass runs each claimed seat's acquire hook inline and a hook can outlast a TTL — started after it, the heartbeat renewed nothing on the boot, so a slow hook lapsed the lease it was building on and the watchdog read the pass as the heartbeat wedged. A RENEW IS SENT A BEAT INSIDE THE HEARTBEAT, on a ticker of its own (`renewPeriod`): `MayStart` admits within a heartbeat of a renew's SENT instant, recorded only once the store answers, so a pass every heartbeat refused a healthy node's seat for every renew's round trip at the shipped TTL, and one counted in received beats slipped by every beat a long pass dropped. A RENEW'S AGE IS THE LARGER OF ITS TWO CLOCK READINGS (`elapsed`), for `MayStart`, `Provable`, `HoldsPresence` and the heartbeat's grace alike: the monotonic reading stops while the host sleeps while the store's TTL runs on, so measured on it alone a node that slept past its TTL woke still proving every lease, and the wall reading alone would let a step back make a renew look younger. A STOP'S COORDINATION STEPS DRAW ON ONE ALLOWANCE (`StopBudget`, one heartbeat interval of the lease TTL, carried on the context and charged only while a step is in flight), so a member that lost its store spends one allowance stopping rather than a bound per step; a layer where a stop may begin takes it with `WithinStop`. `watchdog` is the other half: a wedged event loop cannot be signalled, so the thread's only honest move is to exit — and it is the engine's ONE unilateral exit, so a node whose embedded broker declares itself dead after its boot (nats-server's Fatalf, carried as `jetstream.Death`) leaves through it too (`Watchdog.Fail`), at once and armed or not, because every step a drain would take rides the broker that died — with its OWN exit code (69, `EX_UNAVAILABLE`, beside the wedge's 75), because a restart log is often all a deployment keeps and the two send a reader to opposite places |
| `estate` | The replicated estate ROUTED, from every node — ADR-0025 and its amendment. ONE `Router` runs on EVERY node, with or without `data`, and every seam the tools, the operator's surfaces (the API's tracker, knowledge-base, artifacts and chat routes, the operator's MCP) and the duties consume is answered through it, so a tool cannot tell which kind of node it is on. Every data node holds the whole estate, so the router answers in-process where THIS node's copy serves — held to this node's floors exactly as a remote data node is — and otherwise asks the data nodes its `Placement` names (every live data node, off the WATCHED presence view): the one that answered last first, then a rendezvous order, a node gone silent last for thirty seconds, over ONE subject per serving node rather than a scatter or a queue group, because the asker must know which node an unanswered write went to. A copy that is WRONG is OUT OF SERVICE — a peer asking is told `out_of_service`, and its node keeps its seats while their calls go to peers — and a copy that LAGS its logs is a worse node to ask, never no node: asked last, and told to answer anyway. The failover rule is PER OPERATION CLASS — a read, a tracker write carrying its operation id, a page or chat write carrying its caller's key (`doclet.Actor.OpKey`, `chatlet.Actor.OpKey`; a seat's always does) and chat's erase and prune under the caller's own id move on, from an `unvouched` answer too, under the same id; a page or chat write with no key (whose op id the store mints per call) reported unanswered is `ErrOutcomeUnknown` and never repeated — and every request carries this node's ONE SESSION-FLOOR table, on the operation's OWN domain's log only (a wake's trigger position among them), so whichever data node answers has applied the node's own writes or says it is behind. A read nobody answered, or a walk whose every data node REFUSED, is `ErrUnserved`, never an empty answer — except the knowledge search, which is best effort and answers that it did not run; a repeatable write no data node ran, where some data node NEVER ANSWERED, ends in `ErrOutcomeUnknown` naming the silent ones, because such a node may have run it and "nothing ran" is the claim a caller acts on by writing again — the same operation id is the retry. What cannot cross a wire (the chart seams, the lead map, the searching seat's org) is supplied by the SERVING node's epoch, and three gates hold the wire honest: every field an operation's types carry arrives or is named with who supplies it, every sentinel and error type of statelog and of every domain an operation floors on — the executor log's among them, and the identity estate's own (`iam.ErrNoHolder`, `iam.ErrHolderUnseated`, `iamdomain.ErrNotFound`, `session.ErrNoCompany`) — keeps its errors.Is/As identity across it, and the envelope itself is pinned byte for byte. The IDENTITY reads a node without `data` needs are estate operations too, READ-CLASS and floored on the identity log: who holds a login (its binding row, for `iam.Holders` and so `iam.OwnerOf`) and who holds each human seat (`SeatHolders`, for the party registry's standing and a turn's withheld contacts) — the chart and the asking node's own Tier A token list applied on the ASKING side, since neither is the serving node's to know. CHAT is a floored domain beside them, its search included (the rooms a seat may search are membership rows its own join may just have written): every read crosses with its VIEWER, the seat handle the asking node resolved, inside the signed body, and the SERVING reader decides that seat's rooms from its own membership rows — so a visible set never crosses, and the search resolves it on the node whose index it reads, in one operation that says when that index is still building and names the room each hit was said in, its name and kind from that same set (`ChatSearchAnswer.Rooms`) — while what a caller was admitted to do beyond membership crosses as the asking node's DECISION (`chatlet.MessageAuthority`, `chatlet.ChannelAuthority`), judged on the serving snapshot, and a room's retention is a write of its own rather than a field of a patch; the live channel's room check asks this node's own copy first even while it lags (`Chat.ChannelHere`), never one out of service. THE EXECUTOR LOG is the seventh floored domain (ADR-0073), and its writes are a class of their own: ENGINE-ORIGINATED (`opOrigin`, declared with `byEngine`), the engine's record of what a seat's box did — a capture, an end, the retention's deletes and a removed seat's — which no tool, person or operator surface makes, so `SurfaceWriteDomains` (what `agent/builtin`'s surface declarations are held to) leaves it out, and two gates hold the class: every write on the log is the engine's and nothing else is, and no package serving a person or a model (`agent/builtin`, `api`, `assistant`, `cmd/crewlet`) names the writer facade `ExecutorWrites`, the domain's writer or an engine hand-out of either, by a walk of their source. Each is an idempotent write under the operation the domain derives per execution; its reads (`ExecutorReads`, which a surface may hold) are answered at the caller's level, a launch's conversation read at `linearizable` because a seat that moved holds no floor for the end another node published; and both facades are exactly the domain's method sets, so the engine cannot tell local from routed. A node in a maintenance mode is served every core half's reader and none of its writers. And every request and every reply is SIGNED under the fleet keyring in a domain of its own (`estate`) and judged by the verdicts a state-log record is (ADR-0031), through `signedask` — the one implementation every instruction channel shares, because the server dispatches a write AS the actor, and answers a chat read FOR the viewer, its envelope claims, and an unauthenticated channel beside signed records would let anybody who reaches the cluster port write as anybody: a key the server does not hold answers that the request DID NOT RUN, so the router moves on, and a tampered envelope is refused and logged. A custody batch is an event envelope rather than an estate request, and stays outside the rule |
| `objstore` | The OBJECT STORE — ADR-0026 — and its vocabulary: ONE OBJECT PER UPLOAD, under a `Key` minted for that upload alone — a UUIDv7, strictly parsed, built by `KeyAt` from the store's own clock, carrying the instant it was minted and never minted twice — and an `Object` (key, the whole content's SHA-256 `Hash`, size) that a row names. A key is never derived from the content: content addressing made two files share a stored copy, and a shared copy is one a writer can be re-using at the moment the collector deletes it — so there is NO DEDUPE (a move uploads its bytes again; the seat's own write skips content the file already holds) and NO LOCK. What is divided off is the BYTES and only the bytes: that a file exists, its path, its hash and its object's key are replicated rows like everything else, so listing a project's files never leaves the node. The bytes live in ONE `Backend` the whole fleet shares (`Put`, `Get`, `Stat`, `Delete`, `List`, plus `Pending`/`Abandon` for the uploads it began and never finished, every verb certified by `objstoretest`) — a DUMB, STREAMING store of opaque names: a put reads a reader to its END — `io.EOF` itself, through `Fill`, the one rule every reader in pieces here follows, never a body cut short's `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF`, which `io.ReadFull` answers for its own short read too and so stored the half of an upload that arrived as the whole — and leaves nothing anybody can find when it fails or is abandoned, a get hands back a reader and REFUSES a context with no deadline rather than pick a bound of its own, and a listing hands back every name it holds verbatim and never one it cannot DATE (`ErrUndated`), because what a name means is this package's grammar and the collector's judgement and never a backend's. Every name the engine writes is `files/<key>` (`Key.Name`), so a shared bucket's other objects are never the engine's. It is wrapped in a `Store` that mints the key, counts and hashes every byte on the way in (the size limit and a cancelled context reaching the backend as a FAILED READ, the one failure every backend cleans up after), and on the way out checks every byte against the row and HOLDS BACK THE BYTE THAT COMPLETES THE FILE until the whole is verified — so a download of a damaged object ends short rather than whole, and `ErrCorrupt` is only a DEFINITE answer, never a read that failed first. A read is bounded by `ReadBudget` and ended by `ReadStall` when its backend stops moving; a page (`ReadAt`) is checked for its length only. `RecordWithin` (12 h) is the bound on the write that names a key, which is what lets the collector delete with no lock. `MiBPace` is the ONE floor a transfer is held to on every leg — the API's upload and download pacing, a bucket request, an object read — so no bound can be retuned out from under another. Two OPTIONAL CAPABILITIES a backend may advertise rather than verbs every backend must answer: `Presigner` (the s3 backend, once its probe proved the provider enforces the bindings), through which `Store.PresignPut` signs a client's own put of a key the store minted (`Store.Mint`), bound to length, type, SHA-256 and `If-None-Match: *`, ending inside `RecordWithin` and at most `MaxPresignTerm` (`PresignTerm` sizes it), and answering `ErrDirectUnavailable` everywhere else; and `Reserver` (the nats bucket), whose `Usage` the store reports. `ReferenceTable` is how a consumer DECLARES a table that names objects, its two statements decoded once beside them, and the declarations live in `objstore/references` rather than beside either end, because the collector deletes whatever no declared table names |
| `objstore/natsobj` | The DEFAULT backend: a JetStream object store bucket (`crewlet_files`, stream `OBJ_crewlet_files`) on the fleet's own broker, in NATS's own documented format so `nats object get` reads it, created through `jsprovision` at `stream.replicas` with a BYTE RESERVATION (`store.objects.nats_max_bytes`, or the engine's derived share: the smaller of a quarter of the broker's limit and what the state logs' half and a 1 GiB coordination floor leave), because a bucket growing into the broker remainder the leases and ledgers live in would stop the fleet when full, where a full bucket refuses only uploads (`objstore.ErrFull`); a bucket holding no ceiling is refused naming the `nats stream update` that bounds it, an existing one keeps the ceiling it was granted, `Usage` feeds the bucket gauges and `objects_bucket_near_ceiling`, and `Seal` makes it read-only for good once `crewlet objects migrate --to s3` has moved the fleet off it. It advertises no presign. So the files survive exactly what the logs survive, on the same members, a node without `data` reaches it across its leaf link, and a backup carries it as one more stream snapshot. What it costs is that every member holding a copy holds every object; a company whose files outgrow a member's disk takes `s3obj`. THE PUT IS THIS PACKAGE'S OWN, in the bucket's layout: a FILLER hands it only full `MessageBytes` (128 KiB) messages, at most `putWindow` (8, a mebibyte) are in flight and each window is given `objstore.MiBPace` for its acknowledgements, because the library's put sent a whole object at once and gave each acknowledgement the client's five seconds, which failed a 16 MiB put on slow flushes (spike S7); a failed put waits for what it has in flight, then purges its pieces under a context of its own, and metadata whose acknowledgement never came is read back before anything is purged. EVERYTHING ELSE GOES THROUGH THE STREAM too, because the library's other verbs cannot be made safe: metadata is read from the stream LEADER (`jsapi.Leader`, the one implementation coordination's listings use too) since the library's info read is a direct get a lagging replica answers "not found"; a get is an ordered consumer of the object's own messages read under `ReadStall` and ended by its context — the library's reader holds the lock its close needs, and bounds a context with no deadline by five seconds for the WHOLE download — and a ranged get walks message HEADERS to its offset; a delete is a PURGE of the metadata and then of the pieces, never the library's delete MARKER, which no later put of a never-reused name would replace; `Pending` lists the piece subjects no metadata names. Its listing is an ordered consumer of its own over the metadata subjects, ending on the pending count every message carries — never the library's `List`, which gathers the company's whole inventory into one slice, nor its Watch, which never finishes when the newest metadata cannot be decoded. The bucket is looked up and created ONCE, through `jsprovision`'s `Place`, never through the library's create-or-update, whose UPDATE goes unanswered on a stream whose group has not elected; a bucket held at fewer copies than `stream.replicas` refuses the node. A get or a listing whose consumer the broker took away is replaced within `pullExpiry` (10 s, the shortest expiry that keeps the client's own heartbeat), and a listing pulls `walkAhead` (500) metadata messages at a time |
| `objstore/s3obj` | The S3 backend, over the AWS SDK because SigV4 and the regional endpoint rules are what a hand-rolled client gets wrong: any S3-compatible bucket, keys `<prefix>files/<key>`, path-style for MinIO and most gateways, static keys or the SDK's own chain, `AWS_CA_BUNDLE` added to the roots for the bucket's requests only. Every bucket request goes through `httpx`, never the SDK's own pool, and carries a DEADLINE OF ITS OWN — a fixed one for a request with no body, plus `objstore.MiBPace` a mebibyte for one with a body, five minutes to complete a multipart upload — because `httpx.Client(0)` is right only where every request is bounded some other way; and the bucket is checked at open by a PROBE UNDER THE PREFIX (`<prefix>probe/<key>`) rather than a HeadBucket, which needs a right over the whole bucket and refused the prefix-scoped credential that is the only thing isolating companies sharing one: the probe stores and deletes an object through the engine's own path (failing the boot if it cannot), then proves with presigned puts that the provider refuses a mismatched `x-amz-checksum-sha256` (`BadDigest`) and a second put under `If-None-Match: *` (412). Only a provider that does both offers `PresignPut` (length, type, SHA-256 and the condition all signed) through `DirectUpload`, and only one proven to check checksums gets the header on the engine's single-part puts and a digest on `Stat`. A put streams without knowing its length: under `PartBytes` (8 MiB) it is one PutObject, past it a SEQUENTIAL multipart upload of equal parts through ONE buffer, abandoned under a context the caller's cannot end on any failure, because an upload nobody completes or aborts is billed and listed by nothing — and one a killed process left is what `Pending` (ListMultipartUploads) hands the collector. A whole read sends NO Range header, since an empty object answers `bytes=0-` with 416. `LastModified` of an object put in parts is when its upload BEGAN, which is why `objstore.Info.Written` promises only an instant between a put's start and its end. `s3fake` is an in-process S3 the suite runs against, and it routes on the QUERY before the method, since a multipart upload is spelled with the same methods as a plain put |
| `objstore/memobj`, `objstore/objstoretest` | The in-memory twin and the ONE suite every backend runs (`queuetest`'s idiom): an object reads back exactly, an empty one and large ones across the backend's own pieces and ranges included; a put whose reader fails — with `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF` or a wrapped `io.EOF` too, inside a piece or at its end — or whose context ends leaves nothing a stat or a listing can find, and fails with the reader's own error; a read with no deadline is refused and one whose context ends stops; a missing name is `ErrNotFound`, and a delete leaves nothing and is idempotent; a listing names every object once, verbatim, however slowly it is read, and stops at its visitor's error; and an upload begun and never finished is `Pending` until `Abandon`ed — a twin that agreed only with itself would certify nothing |
| `objstore/collect` | The one fleet duty the object store needs (`object-collector`): an hourly COLLECTION that pins the estate, lists the store and sorts every name by its SPELLING — an object's key under `files/`, or somebody else's, never touched — and deletes a key's object only when it is past a day's grace by BOTH the backend's clock and the key's own minting instant and no declared table names it in an estate read COMPLETELY, with NO LOCK (ADR-0027) — and nothing it CANNOT SEE may: every declared table is its own build's, so it asks the engine first (`Options.Unseen`) and judges nothing while a data node the fleet counts runs a state log this build does not, or a newer build migrated this node's copy of the estate before a rollback, the next build's objects not being this one's to delete; and SWEEPS what no listing shows, the uploads a backend began and never finished (`Pending`/`Abandon`), past the same grace — a sweep the backend refuses is reported beside the collection, never failing it. A daily AUDIT holds the references against the backend's LISTING, a window of 100 000 keys at a time (the smallest above the last window's), walking the references in that window in key order beside it (`Source.Each` from a key, with `ErrSkipTable`), so it holds a window of the inventory and never asks the backend once per object; a reference no listing showed is asked about by name at the end of the pass before it is MISSING, and one held at another size or digest is DAMAGED, each named as the FILE that holds it; both raise `objects_missing`. The report keeps the last audit that ran to its end (`found`) beside the last attempt, and a new duty holder `Restore`s both halves from the fleet's record, so neither a failed audit nor a moved duty clears the alarm. Nothing here decides where an object lives or repairs one — the backend keeps its own copies. The inventory is DERIVED from the estate, never kept beside it, judged 500 keys to a statement over the declaration's own query |
| `objstore/migrate` | One pass of `crewlet objects migrate --to s3`: every object the declared tables name is read verified through `Store.Open` and put into the target backend under the same name, then checked there at its size (and digest, where the target keeps one), `Copies` (4) at a time. Resumable by running it again, because a key never names other bytes and the target is the record of what was copied; an object the source cannot give back whole is reported by file, never stored. It moves no record: the engine's final pass, in maintenance mode, seals the nats bucket and moves the fleet's recorded backend (`coord.ObjectStores.MoveObjectBackend`) |
| `objstore/references` | The ONE LIST of tables that name objects, read by the collector's references and by the backup, and held against the replicated schema IN BOTH DIRECTIONS by a gate: a column named `object` that nobody declared is a table whose files are deleted a day after they were written. It also holds the one test that pins the lock-free rule's arithmetic — `RecordWithin` plus any clock skew under the collector's grace, and the largest file and the largest SEALED executor session (`executordomain.SealedMaxBytes`) at the `MiBPace` floor under `RecordWithin` — because it is the one place every constant is importable. A package of its own because the list belongs to neither the consumer nor the collector |
| `artifacts` | The artifacts VOCABULARY, values only, the way `iam` is the identity vocabulary: ids and the host label (the id's own bytes in lowercase Crockford base32, never a keyed digest, so a keyring rotation cannot change an artifact's origin), kinds, capabilities, the role ladder (viewer, commenter, contributor, editor, owner; a role is a RELATION, never a permission), audiences and shares, manifests and the script policy the linter derives, THE ONE LIMITS TABLE (every bound with its reason, and the Tier A plan quotas a deciding node stamps into a record), the authority stamp a datastore write carries, upload intents, view ticket claims, frames, and datastore op and schema shapes — a room's presence vocabulary is `presence`'s, since a chat room is a room too. Imported by `authz` and `config`, so it imports no store, no broker and no clock. The ordering of roles is `authz`'s, the label codec and id derivation `artifactsdomain`'s, each in a named file of this package |
| `artifacts/manifest` | Pure: the path grammar of a version (the engine's one file-path rule), reserved paths, whole and patch publishes, carry planning and the canonical manifest encoding. CONTENT ADDRESSING LIVES AT THE MANIFEST, never in an object key: keys stay random, one per upload, which is what lets the collector delete without a lock, and a carry never crosses an artifact (a company-wide hash lookup is an existence oracle) |
| `artifacts/mediatype` | Pure: the ONE extension table an artifact may publish (served type, class, compressible, scriptable). The served type is always the table's, never a client's |
| `artifacts/slideformat` | Pure: the slide format a `slides` deck holds (Claude's, without `project/`): the `deck.json` index and one `<section>` per slide on a 1920 by 1080 stage, read with the HTML tokenizer into ONE CANONICAL TREE the publish is held to and the dashboard draws from, so one parser decides what a slide is. A CLOSED SUBSET, REFUSED RATHER THAN DROPPED: the element, attribute and inline-style tables with a value grammar per property, a drawing subset that references only its own ids (`svgsafe`'s allowlist, the one a brand logo is held to), images and faces only as files of the version, Claude's bounds, every finding naming the file, the line and the rule. Its lists are exported for the dashboard's renderer, which a gate holds equal |
| `artifacts/svgsafe` | Pure: the ONE drawing allowlist both an inline slide `<svg>` (read by `slideformat` with the HTML tokenizer) and a standalone `.svg` (a brand logo) are held to: shapes, paths, text, gradients, clips, masks, markers and filter primitives, no script, event attribute, `style`, `<style>`, `foreignObject`, `<a>`, `<image>` or animation, every value free of `javascript:`, `data:`, `<`, `expression` and `@import`, every reference to an id of the same drawing. The standalone front end is encoding/xml in STRICT mode with no entity table, refusing any DOCTYPE or ENTITY, any processing instruction but the XML declaration, `xml:base`, a non-UTF-8 encoding and every namespace but SVG's and XLink's, matching names by their exact SVG spelling, bounding elements, depth and what `<use>` expands to (a use cycle refused), and requiring a viewBox between 1:8 and 8:1. WHAT IS STORED IS ITS RE-SERIALISATION, never the uploaded bytes: attributes sorted, everything escaped, no comment, no instruction and one namespace declaration, so a parser differential between Go's reader and a browser's has nothing to hide in. Refused with the line and the fix, never silently stripped |
| `artifacts/brandformat` | Pure: the format a `brand` kit holds (`brand.json` and the logo and face files it names) and everything an adoption makes of it. The index is decoded STRICTLY (unknown keys, a key twice in any object, a wrong type and anything after the object refused, each finding at its line), every field to its rule (plain text with no control, format character, markup or link, `#rrggbb` colours, exactly one source per face, a licence attestation for an uploaded family). Assets are stored in the engine's form (`Sanitize`: an SVG through `svgsafe`, a PNG read chunk by chunk with its stream inflated row by row to exactly the rows its header promises and metadata chunks dropped, a WebP without EXIF or XMP, a WOFF2 held by its header), and an adoption re-sanitizes what it copies. Contrast is WCAG 2's on the colours the kit paints (`Fallbacks`), only text on the light ground refusing an adoption. THE ONE STYLESHEET GENERATOR (`CSS`): `--brand-*` inputs on `:root` and `@font-face` rules, nothing else, every value re-checked as it is written and every URL `/_crewlet/brand/<sha256>.<ext>`, fuzzed against the injection corpus. Also the canonical index an adoption snapshots, the served extensions, the face lists the dashboard contract is gated to, and the slide theme a deck begun in the brand copies |
| `artifacts/canvasformat` | Pure: a `design`'s `canvas.json`: where each artboard of the version sits (boards keyed by their page's path, with position and size), the order they are drawn in, and the titles and stickies beside them. A CLOSED FORMAT, REFUSED RATHER THAN REPAIRED: decoded strictly (an unknown field is refused, not dropped), every number to its bound, and held to the version so every board is an HTML page of it and every HTML page a board, every problem answered at once at its place in the file. Also the outline the gallery and the agents read and the comment anchor (an artboard point, D65). No runtime of its own: an artboard is a plain page under the html contract (D59) |
| `artifacts/dashspec` | Pure: the format a `dashboard` board holds (`dashboard.json`): filters as closed choice sets, datasets from four sources (`store`, `engine`, `file`, `calc`), widgets drawn by uilet primitives on the twelve-column grid, the ENGINE SOURCE CATALOGUE (named reads over the tracker, spend and agents with typed parameters and fixed columns), the closed calculation steps (deterministic: no clock, no locale, a total order), file datasets parsed and held to their declared columns, refresh, the outline a cover draws and the comment anchor. It reads no store and asks no domain: what answers an engine source, AS THE VIEWER, is the API's (`adr/0043`). A board outside the format is refused at publish with every finding, never dropped on read |
| `jsonform` | The ONE STRICT READER the artifact formats hold their JSON indexes to (a brand kit's `brand.json`, a deck's `deck.json`, a doc's `doc.json`, a design's `canvas.json`, a dashboard's `dashboard.json`), and the one account of each index's shape an agent reads before it writes one: a tool's contract is its validator's, and every fault comes back at once (ADR-0059, whose authority it is). ONE SOURCE FOR THE CHECK AND THE REFERENCE: a `Form` is derived by reflection from the Go type the format decodes into, so the keys and types the strict decode admits are exactly the ones the typed decode reads, and the reference an agent is handed (`Form.Paths`, `Form.Outline`) is the same tree again, with nothing kept in step by hand. EVERY STRUCTURAL FAULT IN ONE PASS: `Decode` names every repeated, unknown and mistyped key, each with the shape the format reads there, and decodes what remains so the format's own rules run in the same pass, because encoding/json with `DisallowUnknownFields` stops at the first, and a brand kit's twelve structural faults were learned one refusal at a time. The strict read is the PUBLISH'S ALONE: a version already stored reads through `Stored`, its format's old acceptance, so nothing stored needs a migration, and a walk refuses the strict read anywhere but the publish pipeline and the formats. A set it names is said through `words`: `OneOf`, the closed set a reference offers, as alternatives (`words.Or`, each member in the backticks the reference writes a value in), and the keys `Form.Outline` panics on, where a reference rules keys its form does not have, as a list read whole (`words.And`), which the format's own test of its reference meets on the change that broke it |
| `artifactsdomain` | The `artifacts` state-log domain (`CREWLET_ARTIFACTS_LOG`), strict and claiming identity, its health NOT a readiness input (a seat reads no artifact on its turn path): artifacts, versions and canonical manifests, the objects each artifact names (`artifact_objects`, refcounted, and `artifact_assets`, both declared in `objstore/references`), shares, pins and opened marks, a year of history and the company's usage counters. EVERY RECORD THAT MOVES AN OBJECT REFERENCE ARBITRATES ON THE ARTIFACT SUBJECT, and the applier writes a carried entry only when its own transaction finds the object named by THIS artifact and an uploaded key only when no other row names it, so an unnamed key is never named again and no carry crosses an artifact. RETENTION RIDES THE RECORD (a hundred unlabelled versions plus every labelled one). QUOTAS ARE STAMPED into the record and enforced by the applier from the stamp. A REFUSAL IS A HISTORY ROW, never an error, and the writer reads its own verdict back by operation id; the applier applies no rule a later build may change (the media table, the grammar and the limits are the writer's). A purge runs its dependents first, step by step. Each committed batch hands the engine what it moved (`Engine.OnArtifactsMoved`), every movement carrying the broker's instant for its record (`Moved.At`), which the socket's change visibility and `crewlet.artifacts.push.lag` are timed from. THE FOLDER TREE IS ONE SUBJECT AND A FOLDER GRANTS NOTHING (ADR-0047): an artifact sits in at most one folder, a folder is seen through its top-level folder's owner and shares, filing an artifact rides its own subject, a removal moves what it held up a level in one record, and the listing (S3 `prefix`/`delimiter`), its total and every folder count share one visibility predicate. THE ADOPTED BRAND IS A SNAPSHOT (ADR-0048): an administrator's adoption on the one `brand` subject copies one kit version's canonical index, its generated stylesheet and every logo and face into objects its own record uploaded, so the kit's retention, trash and purge never reach it; a framed version pins the stylesheet adopted at its publish (naming the brand in its scope), and a superseded adoption's files are pruned once no retained version pins them. Attribution is a seat handle, never a display name. Its read types DECLARE the fields that hold somebody else's words (`untrusted:"fence"` or `untrusted:"name"`, `provenance.FieldTag`), which the agent's read tools may not answer unlabelled |
| `assistantdomain` | The Dashboard Assistant's STATE-LOG DOMAIN on `CREWLET_ASSISTANT_LOG` (ADR-0053): conversations, turns, the messages, steps and cards the person saw, the proposals they decided, parked turns, compliance reads and the FILES a conversation keeps, applied into every data node's replicated estate (replicated migrations 0050 and 0051) because a person opens the drawer on any node. THE SUBJECT IS THE ARBITRATION UNIT: a turn OPENS on its conversation's subject, so one turn runs or waits per conversation whichever node opens it; a turn's park, resume and close are arbitrated on its own; a proposal MOVES on its own subject from the state it was decided from, so two approvals on two nodes produce one (`AlreadyDecided`); messages, steps and cards are ADDITIVE under ids derived from the turn, so a duplicate publish writes one row. A turn's and a proposal's subject id leads with its conversation's, so the purge gate reads it from the subject. Every read is keyed on the OWNER and another person's conversation is not found, never forbidden; the one exception is an administrator's compliance read (D06), which opens a conversation only for the receipt of an audit record already applied beside it, so its owner sees who read it and why. Every value a person said or was shown is SEALED by the writer under the fleet keyring with associated data naming the conversation, the record and the column, and the applier holds no keyring and never opens one. A keyring rotation does not re-seal them: `Reader.SealedKeys` counts them per key (a test holds its columns to the schema's `*_sealed` ones) for the secret store's listing, and the old key stays on the ring until none is left under it. A conversation untouched for its retention (thirty days by default) is purged by the trash reaper (a `trashreap.Termed` source) through its own purge record, decided again at the apply against activity its arbitration cannot see; Clear purges at once; a person's erasure is ONE record taking every conversation they own, published when the identity applier reports the removal and by the reaper's next pass as the backstop; a purge and an erasure install gates. A FILE (an image the person attached, a file a deep-work run delivered) is SEALED INTO ONE OBJECT of the fleet's object store, uploaded BEFORE the additive record naming it is published on the conversation's subject (an attachment exists before its turn), its name sealed in the row and the key its content is sealed under counted by `SealedKeys` from the row; `assistant_files` is declared in `objstore/references` and the domain is an object estate (`ObjectEstate`), so the collector deletes a file's object once a purge or an erasure removed its row. A conversation keeps at most `AttachmentsPerConversation` attachments and `DeliveredPerConversation` delivered files, decided in the writer's snapshot and AGAIN AT THE APPLY, because two additive uploads decided from one snapshot both publish. A barrier, as every identity-claiming log has: the collector's pass pins the estate with it at `linearizable`, and the barrier is the ordinary record the gate reserve and the eviction machinery are proved against on every identity log. A card is validated before it is recorded and never rewritten, so a stored card of a kind this build draws that today's contract refuses was written to another shape (an earlier one, or a newer node's during a rolling upgrade) and is READ AS A NOTICE (`UnshownCardNotice`, ADR-0062), logged by id, kind and reason and counted in `crewlet.assistant.cards.shown_as_notice`, never read field by field; a kind this build does not know passes through. It reads no other domain |
| `executordomain` | The seats' EXECUTOR SESSIONS, `statelog`'s TENTH domain, on `CREWLET_EXECUTOR_LOG` (ADR-0073): one replicated row per (seat handle, turn) whose executor captured its harness's session, naming ONE SEALED OBJECT PER CAPTURE in the fleet's object store under a key the store mints (declared in `objstore/references`, so the collector keeps every object a row names). EVERY RECORD ABOUT A TURN IS ARBITRATED ON ITS CONVERSATION — the seat's handle and a 128-bit digest of the conversation's key, or of the turn's own id for a turn with none — and scoped to it under the seat's path, so the rules over a conversation are decided in log order from one snapshot: its HEAD (the newest ended turn that left a session, read at `linearizable` because the last turn's `end` may be another node's), the ABSORPTION a turn's `end` makes (EXACTLY the turns its first launch's read handed out — the head, the endings it was told, the siblings it was offered — carried on its captures, so what a conversation still holds is what no ended turn was shown, and the next read shows ALL of it, older than the head or not; never "every ended row at or below the read", which a read bounded at sixteen endings would turn into deleting turns nobody was shown) and the work-key DEDUPE (a non-empty key only, the end authoritative for the turn's key, `delivered` merged into the survivor). A capture is refused for an object older than `objstore.RecordWithin`, for an ended or tombstoned turn and for (launch, capture) ordinals not newer than the row's, and the applier repeats every guard but the age; past the seat's byte budget (`turn_engine.conversation_session.max_bytes_per_seat`, read off the fleet's revision by the writer and CARRIED IN THE RECORD so every node applies one number) a capture RELEASES the oldest ENDED sessions' objects, keeping their endings, which is why its scope is the whole seat. A TOMBSTONE IS A GUARD, NOT AN APPLY GATE: everything it refuses is inside the scope of the record that wrote it, so a node that defers that record defers what it decides too, and the eviction is the log's one gate. The object's bytes are streamed through `secrets.StreamSealer` with associated data `executor/<seat>/<turn>/<object key>` (`SessionStore`), and an object that is missing, corrupt or under a dropped key is `ErrUnreadable`, which a restorer reads as no session. Rows leave by the company's conversation retention (the trash reaper's `executor` source, judged off the fleet's revision and never a turn whose run is live — every run record counts, one this build cannot decode included — which takes a row's tombstone with it and first ends as `lost` a turn whose end never came, a day past its last capture), by a removed seat's mailbox retirement (one `seat_delete`, ALWAYS PUBLISHED, whose position every capture and end carries the one its decide saw, so a record decided before a removal never recreates a row for a seat that is gone — the turn no tombstone could name included), by a later turn's absorption and by the budget's release; a person's removal does not reach them yet. Every statement is a named constant, planned by `TestTheExecutorStatementsArePlanned`. Nothing in this build captures or restores — the executor does, through `Writer`, `Reader` and `SessionStore`, which the engine builds on every data node and serves to the estate router, whose `ExecutorWrites` (the engine's alone) and `ExecutorReads` are how a node without `data` reaches the log. |
| `artifactdata` | The artifact DATASTORE as its own strict state-log domain: one subject per store, writes SEQUENCED (no broker expectation; the log orders and the applier decides each write as a pure function of the record and its own rows, all or nothing, the verdict written for every record and read back by its writer). Authority that needs IAM or the chart (role, actor kind, limits) is STAMPED at the API; the applier never reads another domain. Compact integer keys, a running state digest per store and real-byte footprint metering keep later tiering from being a re-derive. A field's last writer is the one the LOG ordered last, never a clock, so two blind writes converge on every data node; a guarded op (`if_version`) refuses the whole batch, and long text is written whole only guarded or appended. Presets compile to the role vocabulary, so no rule is ever evaluated by name. Seven days of history (bounded values, the digest after each row) back cell history and undo, which is a write of the caller's own conditioned on the versions the undone write left. Quotas count growth only, so a write that frees space is never refused. The cost-weighted write budget is PER NODE and answers when it holds the cost again, never a lockout. Per-viewer state is the `private` read rule on the engine's own `_state` collection. What a committed batch moved carries the broker's instant for its record (`Moved.At`), which a datastore push's lag is timed from |
| `artifactdata/canon` | Pure: the ONE canonical document encoding (sorted keys, numbers as decimal text) and the store state digest (a modular sum of document digests) |
| `artifactdata/query` | Pure: the datastore query grammar compiled to SQL over the field index; an unindexed filter is refused naming the fix, never answered by a scan. Every value is a bound parameter, the cursor is a keyset over the order and the document id, and a string longer than the index's 256-byte prefix is rechecked against the row so a prefix match is never answered as an equal one |
| `costlimit` | The one cost-weighted limiter: two token buckets per charge, a principal on a scope and the scope itself, paid together or not at all, PER NODE and never a fleet quota. The datastore weighs a write by the rows its apply writes; a dashboard's live sources weigh a read by the domain read it costs. One implementation so the refill, the forgetting bound and the rounded Retry-After cannot drift between the two |
| `node` | Where the fleet layer becomes runnable — a broker client, a coordination backend and the seat host, composed. Deliberately thin: the one behaviour none of its parts can express alone is that WHEN A SEAT IS ACQUIRED ITS MAILBOX IS ATTACHED, and released in an order that never leaves a seat consuming work it no longer owns. It also owns THE ONE ADMISSION every turn on the node passes, `Node.AdmitTurn` (the seat owned fresh, a slot under `node.max_concurrent`, no drain), EXPORTED because the mailbox's handler is only one caller: the sandbox coordinator's resume asks it too, so it cannot live inside the subscription again, and `TestEveryTurnStartIsAdmitted` fails on a road to `turn.Run` that skips it (ADR-0063) |
| `engine` | The ENTANGLEMENT POINT — which concrete thing satisfies which seam. THE RUNTIME IS TWO HALVES, split by what depends on a company and never by domain (`core.go`, `native.go`): the CORE — the state log for EVERY registered domain, the node gate over every identity-claiming log, the identity estate's two sides (its node writer authoring as the ENGINE, `iamdomain.NodeKind`, so what the node decides for itself is never drawn as a machine's login), the directory trigger over it, and on a publishing node the trim, the identity duties and this node's own usage publisher — is published ONCE AT BOOT ON EVERY NODE THAT HOLDS THE ESTATE (`HoldsEstate`: a node with the `data` role, which `ingress` and `workers` require), company or none, because the node nobody has configured is the one a Tier A token's session signs in on to CREATE its company, and the one its first person is invited to and signs in on once that company declares the human seat they will hold (ADR-0032) — so every node that validates a session reads its own identity rows, with no cache and 503 rather than 401 when it cannot — the SEAT is the one thing that waits for a company, so `SeatView` answers a node running none with the seam's own `session.ErrNoCompany`, which the directory's writer turns into `iamdomain.ErrNoCompany` — `409 no_active_revision` at `/iam`, naming the remedy — rather than a 503 no wait clears, while the request path still reads it as unknown; and it is every domain rather than that one because a join replaces the whole replicated file, a snapshot names every registered domain and the trim counts nodes per log. The usage publisher is the core's although it is no singleton — every data node publishes its own days, from its own event log, whatever its company keeps, and a node without `data` keeps no event log, so its events reach a data node through CUSTODY and are counted in that node's day — because armed with the native half a company on Jira and Confluence never replicated a day of its spend, and a node handed its first company by an apply published none until it restarted. The NATIVE half (Worklet's, Doclet's and Chatlet's writers and readers, the lexical indexer, chat's own keyword index, the change feeds, the embedding duty, the trash reaper and chat's prune duty) waits for the first company that keeps them, and whoever reads it reads `NativeStarted` FIRST, so "not up yet" (503) is never confused with "not this company's" (404); on a node without `data` it starts REMOTE (`startRemote`): the same seams, every one answered by a data node. EVERY ESTATE READ AND WRITE goes through the ESTATE ROUTER (`router.go`, ADR-0025) — a seat's tools, the operator's surfaces and the API's work and page questions alike, on a data node too, whose own copy answers in-process under the same session floors — and the identity reads a node without `data` needs (who holds a login, who holds each human seat) are estate operations like any other, floored on the identity log. Seats are admitted through ONE gate, `StateLogHydrated`: on a data node every core log whose health gates admission (`ReadinessInput`: the tracker's, the knowledge base's and chat's — never the identity log's, the artifacts' two, the Dashboard Assistant's or a compacted one's) established strictly, AND the router serving every half the company runs natively; on a node without `data` the router's word from a data node — and `seatsAdmitted` is that gate behind a mode that publishes. ITS OPENING IS AN EVENT (`admission.go`): every log whose health gates admission tells the engine as it comes level (`statelog.RunnerDeps.OnLevel`), and so does the fleet's view's first listing (`coord.LeaseView.Listed`), and on either, while the gate's last answer to a pass was a refusal nothing has answered, the engine asks the gate again and asks the host for ONE pass the moment it admits — armed by the gate's own refusal, which is an event of its own so an opening that raced the pass is not missed, and never by the host's record of a withheld pass, which a node that cannot serve its seats writes on every pass without asking the gate — so a node that boots behind its logs, or is handed its first company, claims its seats as it comes level rather than at the sweep's next tick; a peer's copy coming to admit is the one opening no local event shows, and the pass every interval asks it. A copy that is WRONG rather than behind is taken OUT OF SERVICE (`judgeCopy`) and the node's seats STAY, reading the estate through another data node — each log's health, a stall on the checkpoint and a deferral's age all feed that judgement — while the identity log is no input to it: an identity fault feeds the vouch watch below and never takes the tracker's, the knowledge base's or chat's copy out of service. The NODE GATE judges an eviction or a readmission in twenty seconds and writes every identity-claiming log AT ONCE in thirty (`GateAnswerBudget`, fifty), and `GateClientWait()` — a minute — is the one wait the CLI and the dashboard are each held to exactly. The teardown ends the claim gate's watch first, since its gate reads the logs, the native halves and the fleet's view everything after it takes down, then the view triggers — the directory trigger and the vouch watch, since neither may outlive what it serves — then the fleet duties and the usage publisher (the native half's embedding writer and chat's prune duty before the core's usage publisher, trim and identity duties, every one before the duty leases are released), then the native half's feeds, indexes and search answerer, and the logs last; a walk over the teardown's calls holds that order. Each domain's change feed (`domainFeed`) carries this node's replicated estate and the domain's runner beside its log, for a translator that decides a wake from its own applier's verdict. Deliberately many small files rather than one large one -- this package is flat, and the hardest passage of all, the inbox handler, is a package of its own (`agent/inbox`); the config apply is `reconcile.go` and `epoch.go` here. THE RECONCILER IS THE ONLY ONLINE WRITER OF WHAT A NODE BOOTS ON (ADR-0057): it moves the boot row only to the revision the engine has just applied (`Reconciler.served`, never the pointer's revision at the applied epoch, which a recreated pointer can reuse for another), repairs it on every converged tick, and holds one lock per engine from its read of the pointer to that write (`Engine.following`), because two ticks interleaved let the one that read the older epoch apply last and walk the boot row back. It keeps the fleet's revision in the store's fleet row before validating it (`Reconciler.keep`), since a refused revision is still the company, and its apply record names what the engine serves (`Reconciler.Serving`) and the epoch it last APPLIED beside the one it attempted (`Reconciler.Applied`, `config_applied_epoch`, because a refusal says the node did not reach the epoch, never which one it did), so a node that refuses the fleet's revision keeps running the one before, and says so in the fleet view. It moves the boot row on EVERY tick — converged, out of retries and before a retry alike, because a node that refuses the fleet's next revision never converges again and the tick out of retries is the only one left to repair a write that failed — and the record carries the boot row as the reconciler last READ or WROTE it (`Reconciler.Boots`, `config_boot_revision_id`) rather than what the node serves, because the write is local and best effort and the fleet must say which revision the node would actually come back on. That name is never unknown: `NewReconciler` reads the row before it returns and fails when it cannot, as the boot fails on a store it cannot read, and a read that fails on a later tick leaves the last answer standing — published as none, an unread row was a node that boots on nothing to the reader keeping what it needs to start. It moves only on a read or a write that succeeded, so the record never names a boot revision ahead of the store whatever the order; the boot row moves BEFORE the record only so that the record says a move on the tick that made it. A node whose own database is scratch reports none, because the row it writes is deleted by the start that would read it — learned from the store handle (`store.DB.Scratch`), never from an option a caller could leave at its zero value. `mcp.go` is where `mcp_servers:` becomes running children: shared servers on the epoch, per-role children on a seat's LEASE, each claimed seat with its own bridge and its own registry — two children of one template publish the same tool names, so anything shared between seats hands one seat's identity to another. `seatservers.go` keeps those per-role children CONVERGED while the seat is held, through ONE RUNNER PER SEAT (the claim, an apply and a retry each ask it to go again rather than race it): every apply hands the seat's runner a reconcile against the new epoch and secret snapshot (a moved spec restarts, a missing child starts, a failed start retries from fifteen seconds doubling to five minutes, and a seat the published company no longer declares has every child retired by that pass rather than at its release, since placement may hold it a whole sweep interval longer) WITHOUT the apply waiting on it, and no pass starts a child unless `seat.Host.Provable` says the lease is still this node's — a child started once at the claim never saw a credential provisioning sealed a moment later, and its agent heard messages it could never answer. `toolcredentials.go` is the rule ANY tool server is STARTED by at all, in ONE function every start passes (`resolveServer`, the only source of a spec's values and of a box's): every `${VAR}` the server is started with — its own `env` or `headers`, its `url`, and a seat's own `mcp_env` block for it, merged over them key by key BEFORE anything resolves and by the key the server READS, so an http header is one header in any case — must resolve first, because a vendor's server handed an empty token refuses to start or acts as nobody — one a provisioning pass mints for the seat, read off that pass's seat plan (`seatMinter`), which is the plan its own run mints by and the only one this package reads (a walk holds it), WAITS unstarted and not failed until the seal reaches the node; one that pass's last report says it cannot mint until a person acts FAILS with that finding, judged again on every heartbeat since a seat's pass never sees an integration change; one nothing mints FAILS naming the variable and its remedies — the integration's provision command only for a seat's own credential, and the `optional_keys` declaration only for a server's own setting, never for a seat's identity or a variable the url needs; none is retried on a timer, because every change to the snapshot runs the seat's pass and an apply's reconcile of the shared servers — a shared server held back is never handed to its bridge, whose supervision would retry it — and a running server whose credential stops resolving is stopped; and the same rule withholds the server from a harness's box, and holds a coding run's OWN environment and setup files (`runEnvironment`, `sandbox.go`, each declaring its own `optional_keys`): an executor's run is the turn itself, which a refusal would fail, so it starts without what is missing and says so (`sandbox_env_unresolved`). Every turn's tools are served from ONE tool-session host per process (`toolsession.NewHost`, made at boot and closed at shutdown), refused at boot naming `TMPDIR` when its socket paths would not fit. `bridge.go` and `agentrun.go` are where a run reaches the seat's tools: the launch opens the run's bridge session under its JOB (`LaunchRequest.Opened`), fenced on the seat for every call, and records the NODE that launched it on the run's row; a run a recovery finds still running has its session re-opened from the run's own row (`reopen.go`), its surface rebuilt from the parked turn and the calls already logged, so its box's token resolves on the node that holds the seat after a restart or a move; every node's bridge joins the fleet's relay (`bridgefleet.go`) — an ingress node with `api.port` 0 excepted, which serves nothing a box could dial — and is told when a seat is taken (after its runs are recovered) and when it is handed on. A direct box dials its own node's listener on loopback wherever that node runs seats and binds a listener loopback reaches (`Options.BoxHostURL`, which the bridge and the telemetry receiver are both built from, the receiver only where the node forwards to an upstream) and every other box `CREWLET_MCP_BRIDGE_URL`, the fleet's address, and every cell a launch can reach that its bridge cannot is said once at apply (`executor_bridge_unreachable`, `advisories.go`). `sandbox.go` resumes a parked turn (`parkedTurnRunner`, which the reopen builds its runner with too) — with a person's answer CARRIED ON in the run's own session where its box was kept (`runner.Asked`), and a resume the budget refuses HELD on its window (`budgetHold`, `sandbox.HeldError`) rather than abandoned; a completion's turn gate asks NO budget and NO limit its models know (`seatCannotRun`), so a run that finishes on a full window, or on a plan another seat's run just spent, is collected and charged in its window, its box paused, before its review is held. A resume that meets its models' limit PARKS its seat as a dispatch does (`parkResumeOnLimit`: `seat_provider_parked`, the chain's probe), since a plan is usually spent inside a run whose seat no delivery reaches, and so does a collected completion whose review's chain already knows the limit (`collectedRefusal`, before its turn runs), which is also what parks a seat whose mail its run holds again after an apply carries its entry's sit-out; and a seat parked on its provider here admits NO resume until the park is released (`providerParkRefusal`, at the turn gate, naming the probe's next call only while it is still to come), whichever chain the resume's first call takes: past its own hold's instant, a completion whose review had sent it back to spent runners was otherwise reviewed and relaunched into the vendor's window every hour. A PARK OUTLIVES NO SEAT: a seat's release waits for no turn that can park it, so a park taken after the release forgot the seat's parks asks for the seat once it is taken and undoes itself (`budgetPark`, `providerPark` through `unparkReleased`, which leaves the probe and says the seat released even where the forget got to its record first), and the forget lifts the hold of a park taken between the release's detach and itself (`seatParks.forget`): left, its probe published a seat parked that a peer ran, and its hold kept the inbox shut on this node with no park to lift it. A park's NOTICES go through `parkNotices` (`parknotice.go`): once per change, the newest per seat, published by the caller while the broker answers, and held and retried on a one-to-thirty-second backoff while it refuses, said once when the outage starts and once when they land, where each renewal of each seat was once published on the spot and logged as it failed; and a probe refused with a next try already past waits `harness.ShortestSitOut` rather than asking again at once. A host setting passed through by name that may be unset is DECLARED, by KEY, in the server's `optional_keys`, and a key so declared whose value does not resolve is LEFT OUT whole — never passed empty, never half a value — except a variable a pass mints for the seat, which is waited for whatever the declaration says; not spelled `${VAR:-}`, which the reference grammar deliberately does not parse. `mcpsupervise.go` is what the engine decides about the bridges' own supervision: a per-role child that DIES is restarted by its bridge only while `seat.Host.Provable` holds — the predicate the passes ask — and a pass leaves it to that bridge (`mcp.Bridge.Runs` answers yes for it) rather than starting a second copy, so the runner and the supervisor never run two loops over one child; a shared server whose start failed is retried by its bridge, and what it brings back is refiled into the CURRENT epoch's registry — the one part of a published epoch that changes — and every held seat's surface. It is also where every fleet-singleton DUTY is armed, and the reason is the same: the trim, the maintenance sweep, the EMBEDDING duty and chat's PRUNE (`chatretention.go`, a record carrying a cutoff the broker's own instants are compared with, so every data node deletes one set) each need the company's provider, the log's publisher and the fleet's leases in one place. The org chart's projections are here too and are NOT duties — the tracker's projects, the knowledge containers and the unit ROOMS (`chatunits.go`) follow every published company on every node, because each create arbitrates on its own subject and the loser is told it is held. The alarm table is the exception that proves it — evaluated on EVERY node on its own HEARTBEAT (`statelog.AlarmInterval`, which IS `statelog.PositionHeartbeat`, the ten seconds every data node republishes its position on — one declaration, because a copy of the number per package agreed only until one was retuned) and never behind the trim's duty claim, because a reading describes one NODE and a table evaluated only where the lease sits would report the lease holder's health as the fleet's, and because the trim's quarter-hour silently raised every sixty-second threshold to fifteen minutes. The trim's tick is where the SCANS the table needs are taken — the vector coverage and each log's daily intake, the second withheld through a log's first two days because its first day is the one a company imports into — and so is the store pool's wait DELTA, which whoever reads it clears for everyone after, so it is taken on that regular quarter-hour alone, the cadence the `pool_wait` p95 is a percentile of; the heartbeat and every report read them back rather than repeating them; a gauge whose measurement became unknown is WITHDRAWN rather than left holding the last figure. Whether the trim floor can be read is observed on the heartbeat itself (`floorwatch.go`), through the read path's own two steps — one listing, then `floorFor` per domain — so `floor_unknown` lists exactly the floors every read refuses on, one log each in its `domains`. A binding dangles by ONE rule, `bindings.go`, which is the request path's own seat table applied to a directory row, and `/iam/check` is the one place it is reported, with the repair its holder's KIND takes — a person is moved to another human seat or removed, since the directory refuses to unbind one, and a service account unbound or moved — never an alarm, because it is a fact about the company's content that every node would raise at once and none could repair (ADR-0015). The IDENTITY estate's duty is armed here too (`identityduties.go`) — the per-bucket sweep publisher — on its own lease of three of its hourly intervals, inside `coord.MaxDutyTTL`. It is armed only where the `workers` role runs, although every data node runs the identity domain — elsewhere every claim would be refused, so arming it would be a loop that never runs reported as a duty that does — and `/health` serves what a node armed as `identity_duty_seconds`, because a duty never armed looks exactly like one finding nothing. And the COMPANY KEY a blind is derived under (`companykeys.go`) is MINTED here, on first use, with a CREATE the store refuses over a key that landed first, and every minter reads back whichever did: the store is last-write-wins for a put, and two nodes that each found a key absent each read their OWN write back and derived under different keys. The fleet hold and the in-process gate stay only as the courtesy that keeps two nodes from each running the guard — a lease cannot fence a holder paused past it, so a put under the hold split the company exactly as it did with none. A mint takes a REQUIRED guard, and the identity key's refuses both an estate holding a blind (minting over a DELETED key orphans every address) and rows that have not APPLIED the whole identity log — behind it, or holding a record they retained, which moves the checkpoint without writing the rows — whose "no blind here" may be rows that were never applied; the log's END is read first (`IdentityLogEnd`) and the rows prove themselves against it in their own snapshot. A NODE THAT STOPS VOUCHING FOR ITS IDENTITY ROWS SAYS SO (`identityvouch.go`): every identity read compares the identity log's lag against `statelog.StallGrace`, and an applier halted on a record it cannot read, or frozen behind a broker, commits no batch for any listener to hear — so a watch over that same lag hands the credential listener a move naming everyone the instant it crosses the grace, once per crossing, which is the instant the request path starts answering 503 — and that move only closes the node's sockets, whose reconnects the handshake decides. The party registry has TWO rebuild triggers — a published company, and the identity applier's `Moved.Seats` (`directory.go`), with a thirty-second re-read behind it, which is the only trigger beside a published company on a node without `data`, where who holds each seat is read through the estate router (`SeatHolders`) — serialised on one lock, each building a WHOLE registry for its company and swapping it, never a diff; a directory it cannot read carries the last reading forward, and a node that has NEVER read one withholds every human seat until it does, because the reading it would otherwise carry is the chart-only one. WHOSE CREDENTIALS a committed identity batch moved (`iamdomain.Moved`, `identitymoved.go`) goes to whatever `SetOnIdentityMoved` registered — the API, which decides again the dashboard sockets a move names and closes every socket on a move naming nobody, since every data node applies the identity log and so closes its own, and every node serving the API is one — and an adoption or a reopened estate, which put a donor's rows in place with no committed batch to name a revocation the donor applied, say `Everyone` (`stateLog.estateReplaced`).
