The appliers' post-commit hooks are one `applyHooks` value built by `Engine.applyHooks`, so the register's own cases build appliers from what a running node hands them — a nil hook compiles and passes every case in the domain's own package. The FOUR hooks that announce to sockets — the tracker's inbox movements, chat's committed batches (`SetChatLive`, the hub the process attaches after `New`), and what the artifacts' and the datastore's commits moved (`OnArtifactsMoved`, `OnArtifactDataMoved`, which the artifact origin's verdict cache hears too) — go through ONE LIVE RELAY (`liverelay.go`): a node whose copy is out of service hears a sound peer's appliers instead of its own, every domain switched at once because the verdict is the whole copy's, one ring bounded by its encoded bytes rather than its batches, and every switch a re-read in every domain (to the artifact listeners, their domains' own reset), and every ask and answer SIGNED on a `signedask` channel of its own (`live.relay`, built once in `New`, the relay refused rather than served unsigned): it only reads, but a follower pushes what it is answered to every socket it holds as a peer's appliers' work — chat excerpts and mentions, datastore documents whole — so an unsigned answer was anybody on the broker showing words in a room under anybody's name, and an answer the follower cannot believe is passed over while it waits for its server's own, since the transport names no sender and a forged reply that ended the ask would move every follower off every server. The artifact copies' own follower of that verdict, which withheld this node's artifact pushes and relayed nothing, is gone: a second reader of one fact on its own clock left an out-of-service node's artifact rooms hearing nobody. Every fleet decision that destroys, by the company document, what no undo restores is made only on a node that has applied the revision the fleet's activation pointer names (`maintenance.go`, `fleetCompany`): the mailbox sweep's roster judges a seat ABSENT there and nowhere else, so a node behind on an apply never stamps a seat the fleet has just added, and the clearing of a removed seat's pause, the forgetting of a removed human seat's chat read state, chat's PRUNE horizon and the assistant's retention term are read the same way, so none of them deletes by a revision the fleet has replaced. THE ORG IS THE APPLIED EPOCH'S: `roles:` and `units:` are part of every revision, built into the running `org.Organization` once per apply, and ONE list (`converge.go`, `followCompany`) brings everything derived from a company up to the published one — the party registry, the seats' vendor identities and tool surfaces, the tracker's projects, the knowledge containers and the unit rooms (stamped with the revision's activation instant), the mailboxes, the scheduler and the socket push — run by boot and by every apply, so a step added to one path is a step both get. Every HOLD on a seat's mailbox — a person's pause, a budget park waiting out a spent window — is keyed on the seat's ID (`seatpause.go`, `budgetpark.go`), because the inbox a hold sits on is named by the id. The A2A ANSWER LEG is the engine's (`a2a.go`): a colleague's ask is answered by whatever ends the turn that took it up, off the trigger or off the run's record, a lost run's included (`answerLostTurn`, the coordinator's `TurnLost`); and so is the idle sweep's question whether a quiet channel is still OWED (`a2aowed.go`, `colleagueOwed`): its ask on a live run's record, or waiting in its seat's inbox held by a run, a person's pause or a budget park, the same four readings the seat's own holds are made from. A pause with `stop_running` also ENDS the seat's running coding run, on the node that holds the seat — when the pause lands, on every complete read of the pauses and when a stopped seat is acquired (`runstop.go`), whose recovery re-opens no session for the run it then ends (`reopen.go`), nor for one whose box went with a node the fleet found gone — and closes the turn parked on it as stopped under identities derived from the run's ending (`closeStoppedTurn`), while a launch the stop beats to its park closes its own turn as stopped, and a resumed turn whose call comes back refused for a limit or the budget as the stop lands is settled as stopped rather than reverted to wait and parked (`resumeWaits`, `sandbox.go`); a turn's park record is published BEFORE its park is written, so anything that ends the parked turn is newer than the park. And it is where every FLEET-WIDE consumer group — one any node may attach, so a message goes to whichever member takes it first — declares whether its handler needs data (`groups.go`): `joins` is the one door every attachment passes, a node without `data` joins none that do, and a gate holds the table against the tree's subscriptions in both directions, because a stateless member of a group that reads the estate answers its share of the messages from nothing and nothing fails. And it is where the three INSTRUCTION CHANNELS the engine serves beside the estate are signed (`fleetbroker.go`, `steer.go`, `sandboxhost.go`) — a broker member removing a voter through its own system account, the node running a turn taking a person's note as that person, and a box's host reading a run's box back, and pausing it, for the node collecting the run — each a `signedask` channel built ONCE in `New` from the fleet keyring on every node and refused rather than served unsigned, because each carries out what it is asked on the asking node's decision, and the broker authenticates nothing: a member runs only what it admits, a key it does not hold is "did not run" so the next member carries the removal, and the carrying member's log names the author, kind and credential read off the signed request; `TestEveryInstructionChannelSignsInDomainsOfItsOwn` holds every channel's key domains apart from each other's and every state log's (the live relay's, the presence seal's, the box bridge's relay's, the assistant's and the seals a coding run's record carries among them). A note to a turn whose coding run is out is NOT a scatter (`runsteer.go`, ADR-0067): it waits SEALED on the run's record in the coordination store (`seat.steer.note`, beside a sealed seat anchor, `seat.steer.seat`), filed by any node with one compare-and-swap, served to the run's harness by the inbox its bridge session carries — built from the record alone, so a node that reopens the session builds the same one — read by the launch's brief and the review, carried in the order the record NUMBERED the writes that read them (`RunSteer.Reads`) rather than any node's clock, and expired only by the run's ending (`CoordinatorOptions.SteersExpired`) — every note no job ran with, the brief of a launch that failed after its write included, so nothing else reports one; the desk HANDS a first segment's box over IN THE WRITE THAT CREATES THE RECORD (`steerDesk.handOver`, `LaunchRequest.HandedNotes`), holding an offer that arrives meanwhile until that write lands, and files a late offer there, so a note to a running turn is never answered `closed` nor filed ahead of one the box took first. ONE READING OF TIER A'S STREAM BLOCK (`streamConfig`) and ONE FLEET-STORE CONSTRUCTOR (`openFleet`) serve the boot and `OpenFleetStore` alike — the fleet's coordination store reached by a process that is not the engine (a `crewlet` command while the engine is stopped): a solo member starts its own broker on its store directory, under that directory's lock and with no leaf listener; a node on an external broker dials it; a member of a cluster (no quorum alone, and rejoining as this node for one value is a restart's cost) and a node that holds no data (a leaf of the members') are `ErrNoFleetStoreHere`. Only the fleet store: not the queue, which would provision the engine's streams, nor the lease bucket or the object store, whose settings the engine decides. A `direct` or `container` box is its launching node's (`sandboxhost.go`): every node runs a waiter of its own for the boxes on its host beside the fleet's, answers a peer's signed read of one of them (`sandbox.box_read`, only what the run's row licenses) and listens for the nudges (`crewlet.sandbox.nudge`) its waiters act on — a run's `submit_work` recorded by the bridge ledger, a box left to its host — and the fleet's waiter reads the presence leases to find a node gone for good. A node's MODEL STATE is gathered for its heartbeat (`modelstate.go`: each entry's sign-in plan, the parks naming it, the login watcher of `hostlogin.go`) and its executor cells probed (`runnerprobe.go`); a person's retry is a coordination record every node applies by its stamp (`modelretry.go`), a device sign-in one record per model whose process runs on one node (`devicesignin.go`), and a run refused on a sign-in replaced while it ran is carried on once (`signInReplaced`). The model alarms' inputs are fleet-wide (`modelalarms.go`). WHAT ITS MCP SERVERS LIST is derived off its bridges at every beat and written only when it moved, under its presence epoch, schemas first; every node keeps a watched copy of the fleet's listings, which the tool catalogue adds a per-agent server's tools from and which a server's detail reads, and the maintenance sweep removes what no reader counts (`mcplistings.go`, ADR-0074). |

## The agent runtime

| Package | What it is for |
|---|---|
| `agent/turn` | The turn loop — an executor and a reviewer — the guards around them, and the one question the whole engine turns on: did this turn actually reach anybody? WHO IS WAITING is derived at dispatch from the trigger's own type and WHAT RAN is the tool loop's own record, so neither half is something a model can get wrong. It replaced a reconciliation of the planner's PREDICTED tools against reality, which is where both recorded delivery incidents came from |
| `agent/runner` | Drives the phases against real models and real tools — the wiring between the prompt builder, the tool registry, the provider chain and the turn loop's contract, none of which knows the others. Plumbing, plus the one rule plumbing cannot express: what happens when the model never gives a structured answer at all. What a valid answer IS lives here too, in the decoders; the SHAPE is `agent/structured`. Every phase is a harness's own loop over the seat's tools, and every phase but the executor runs on this host, served from the process's one tool-session host (`Config.Sessions`, required — a runner with none is refused before any turn). The executor is ONE runtime (`agentrun.go`): its harness's own agentic run in a box — the launch, which parks the turn, and the collection, which replays the run's bridged calls, in the order the bridge admitted them, through the run's surface rebuilt as the run left it to recover the submission it made, with the same prompt, the same surface, the same submission and the same rescue as every phase, and only the rounds happening in the harness. A person's answer to a run's question CARRIES THE RUN ON (`asked.go`, `Resume.Asked`) rather than being reported to the turn: the run continues its own session in its kept box, or starts afresh told the question, the answer and its branch. A reply in the conversation it asked in reaches it inside a fence of the relaunching turn's token, labelled as the reply's trigger labels the message (ADR-0040); an answer given by turn reaches it defused and unfenced, as the instruction of the person who gave it. So does a job LOST with its host (`lost.go`, `Resume.Lost`): the same round relaunched in a fresh session, told it was cut off, why and where to look for its work — unless the round's calls already hold its submission, which collects it — and refused past its limit with `ErrRoundLost`, closing the round's phase failed. A node that recovers a run still RUNNING — after a restart or a move — rebuilds its surface from the turn's parked state and the calls already on the row (`ReopenAgentRun`), so the next call's operation id carries on from them. Every segment that re-enters a run — its collection, an answer or a loss carrying it on, a reopen — makes ONE rebuild (`rebuiltSurface`): the tools and skills the launch recorded, then every tool the run activated and every skill it loaded over the bridge, and the turn's call log seeded with the run's calls. The collection calls nothing through its surface, and is rebuilt the same way all the same: its own rebuild took the launch's record alone, and its record published every tool the run had activated as one it could still discover. And the surface's own METER (`BridgedSpend`), because the harness calls the seat's tools after the segment that launched it has charged what it spent, so what those calls cost (the auxiliary rewrites, the delegated workers) is counted apart, written to the run's row with every bridged call and paid by the segment that resumes. It hands the extension judge the turn's fence token (`extension.Request.Boundary`), so the judge can rekey the task's fences onto a token of its own call, and writes the judge's verdict back into the conversation only through `extension.Nudge`, which defuses the judge's reason under that same turn token — a reason is a model's words about other people's words, and spliced in raw it could close a fence the seat reads. A person's NOTES reach a phase through ONE SOURCE (`SteerSource`, `Config.Steer`): the first segment's box, or the run's record a resumed segment reads — taken at a round of a host phase, PEEKED for a run's brief (`Brief`), whose launch records them read and publishes them delivered once it has launched (`Launched.LaunchID`), and given back when the launch fails BEFORE ITS WRITE (`Return`) — a launch that fails after it names the job it opened, and the record's ending reports them; a resumed segment opens with every note the record says the turn read (`Resume.Steered`), and the execute record lists its round's reads at round 1 with the kind of step — every job's of the round, the asking job's included where an answer carried the run on, as it lists that job's calls (`runReads`) Three kinds of round are CARRIED ON rather than collected: one a person's answer drives (`Asked`), one lost with its host (`Lost`), and one refused on a sign-in replaced while it ran (`Rotated`, once). |
| `agent/runner/steersourcetest` | The ONE contract suite every `runner.SteerSource` runs (`queuetest`'s idiom): the first segment's box (`steer.Box`, run from `agent/steer`) and the record of the coding run a resumed segment reads (`internal/engine`'s source over the run's record, run from there) — two homes of a turn's notes the runner reads alike. The shared promises only: a waiting note is taken once, by one take, however many race for it; `Waiting` reports a note WITHOUT TAKING IT and never waits on a store, since the tool loop asks it under its dispatcher's lock; and a brief's notes are never lost and never reported twice — handed back after a launch that failed before its write, and taken again by the next reader; or, where the launch had written them read by a job that never ran, or another node's ending reached the record first, recorded expired exactly once, by the record's ending alone. A source with no record to write a brief on, or no store to stall, leaves out only the part of a case that needs one (a return, never a skip `internal/skipgate` would refuse); the box's bounds and the record's seals and fences stay in their own packages' suites |
| `agent/structured` | How a phase gives a typed answer: it CALLS A TOOL rather than writing prose the engine parses, so the ordinary tool loop carries the answer out and nothing needs a side channel. Three rules travel with the shape — a rejected submission goes back to the model (the one failure a model reliably fixes), the last write wins (a second submission is a correction), and an absent submission is not a value (every rescue path turns on telling "decided this" from "nothing decided anything"). A submission tool is SAFE FOR CONCURRENT CALLS, because a harness may make a response's calls at once and a bridged box's calls arrive on their own requests. The decoder belongs to the caller, and `Remarshal` — the one mapping from arguments to a typed payload, which `delegate` decodes through too — REFUSES a field the payload does not have, at any depth and by name: decoded the obvious way it was DROPPED, so a reviewer's misspelt `completed_work` never told the next round what had already landed, and a delegated task's `afer` ran at once on input it was told to wait for |
| `agent/phase` | The per-phase vocabulary: which phase is running, which model it runs on, and what a turn concluded. `execute` names the phase although it now decides as well as acts: deciding is part of the seat doing its own work, which is what every dashboard and rollup that reads the value means by it. Which entries a seat's phase runs on is ONE resolution (`Resolve`), and `ResolveWithSource` is the same walk also saying which level decided it (`seat`, `executor`, `default-entry`, `first-entry`), so a page that shows where a job lands reads the engine's answer rather than a copy of its precedence |
| `agent/toolloop` | One phase's model-and-tools conversation, and every rule a call is held to — THE HARNESS DRIVES THE ROUNDS; THE LOOP HOLDS THE PHASE (ADR-0049). Every model call is a harness running its own loop, so `Run` starts HARNESS RUNS of the phase's provider, each served the phase's tools by a DISPATCHER (`dispatcher.go`, the `llm.ToolHost`; each run's session hands its calls to a view bound to that run, `runCalls`, so a call is the run's that made it and a run already released refuses one) that decides each call as it arrives: the grant and the skill guard (the `Surface`), the fence before every run and before and after every call, each request charged as the harness's record shows it and a full window asked about only where the harness would ask its model again (never as a call is admitted, so a submission in the request that filled it still finishes the phase), the round cap (a round is one model request; the last allowed round's answers are WITHHELD and the run ends `exhausted`), a terminator or a call the surface marks detached (`Detacher`, which only the Dashboard Assistant's surface implements) run ALONE after its response's other calls — waiting only for calls the harness's declared `Concurrency` could be making beside it, since a harness that makes them in turn sends none while one is held — a call near the harness's own tool timeout answered "still running" so the run ends and the next one is handed the result, a person's note ending the run once its response's calls are answered so the NEXT run reads it as a USER message (never inside a tool result, which any tool's output could imitate), an activation on a harness that does not re-list ending the run, a panic cutting the phase, unparsed arguments answered and never run on `{}` (`UnparsedArguments`, exported so the bridge answers in the same words), and a call named other than the model asked — Hermes rewrites names — refused. Calls run under the PHASE's context, never the MCP request's, and the surface fixes each call's place in the run's call log AT ADMISSION (`Surface.Admit`), because a harness's calls return in whatever order their tools finish. Once a run is ending, every answer the harness would act on is withheld, so it makes no request it would be billed for. The FOLD (`fold.go`) turns each run back into the record the loop kept when it drove each round itself — assistant messages with their calls, tool results, `Execution`s with `CallID`, `Narration`, `Round`s — plus one `Invocation` per harness run (`end`, `cap`, `stopped`, `failed`) and a LIST of `Running` calls, so nothing downstream tells the two apart except by the runs it lists; a harness whose record shows no requests counts as one round per run. Between runs it does what only the loop can: a note, a corrective (a phase that finishes by a call is ASKED AGAIN when a run ends without it, `TerminateAfter`), a call's result the run ended before. And its CLOCK — every request and every tool call is timed here — and the `Suspend` primitive the Dashboard Assistant parks a turn on a proposal through (`Config.AllowSuspend`, which no seat phase sets). Every phase the ENGINE drives on this host is this loop — the reviewer, a delegated worker, the onboarding pass, the assistant's quick mode — and a seat's executor is NOT: it is its harness's own run in a box, reaching the seat's tools over the bridge one call at a time (`api/mcpbridge`), whose record the runner reads back from the bridge's call log |
| `agent/steer` | A person's note to a RUNNING turn: the box one turn holds and the wire a note crosses. IN THE ENGINE'S OWN LOOP a note enters the conversation as a USER message at the OPENING OF A harness RUN, IMMEDIATELY AFTER THE FENCE: one that arrives while a run is going ENDS that run once the calls of the response it arrived during have their answers (`Box.Waiting`), and the next run reads it. INSIDE A CODING RUN, which has no round of the engine's to open, it is read in the run's brief and at the harness's own boundaries between its steps (below) — at a point where every call has its answer either way, so a note can never split a call from its answer; the engine never folds one into a tool's result any tool's output could imitate (where a harness then places a hook's context is its own — on some models Claude Code appends it to the end of the last tool result — so a run's standing instruction tells a note by its headings, never by where it sits: `sandbox`), and never into the system prompt a provider caches on. The reviewer and every phase of the engine's own loop read notes; a worker, the onboarding pass and the judge do not. A turn whose executor's coding run is out keeps its notes NOT IN A BOX BUT ON THE RUN'S RECORD (internal/engine, runsteer.go, ADR-0067): the box is the first segment's only, until the launch hands what it holds over IN THE WRITE THAT CREATES THE RECORD (`Box.HandOver`'s notes ride it, waiting, ahead of any note filed there directly; `Box.Reopen` gives them back to a launch that failed before the write, and a launch that fails after it leaves them to the record's ending), and from there a note is read in a run's brief (`Box.Brief`, `Return` for a launch that failed before its write), between the run's steps through its bridge, or by the review — so the SOURCE a runner reads (`runner.SteerSource`) is a box or the record, never both. A read note is CARRIED into every later phase of the turn — from the run's record, which holds every read, when a segment resumes it — or the reviewer grades against the task the person corrected. A turn takes at most `MaxTurnNotes` (10) notes in all, answered `spent` past that, beside the five that may wait (`MaxPendingNotes`); an accepted note answers WHEN it is read (`Reads`: `next_step`, or `after_answer` for a run parked on a question). `Record` is the ONE constructor of `agent_turn_steered`, which every publisher calls — the runner, a run's bridge inbox and an ending — with the kind of step that read a note (`types.SteerBoundary`) and the run's job. The note is scattered on the queue's ephemeral verb, SIGNED and bound to one request on `signedask`'s `seat.steer` channel — the running node offers it as the person it names on the asking node's decision, so a note the fleet did not sign reaches no turn and an answer the asker cannot verify is no answer — and only the node running the turn answers — it remembers the last 256 turns it ended so a note that raced the end is `closed` rather than silence — and a retry of one request is one note, because the note's id IS the call's operation — the request's `Idempotency-Key` as `internal/api/opkey` scoped it to its principal — so an MCP call, which names none, is a new note every time. What became of it is `agent_turn_steered`: `delivered` where it was read, by the node whose step read it, or `expired` — by the node that closed the box, or that finished the run's ending — when the turn ended first. WHO MAY STEER is decided on the turn's SEAT — its lead, or `admin` — which a turn whose run is out names on its record under a SEALED ANCHOR, read by the asking node itself with no scatter; before that the node a request reached cannot know that seat until the running node says: `admin` admits at once and a caller who leads nobody is refused at once, while anybody else's call first sends a PROBE that offers nothing (one nobody answers is `unavailable`) and is decided again on the seat it names before the note is offered; the sender travels as `iam.ActorFor`'s author, kind and credential, never as a second bound-seat field beside them that could name somebody else |
| `agent/execstate` | The wire format for a turn PARKED on its executor's run. The run is detached in a box and outlives the process, so the turn is re-entered when the run is collected — after a restart, possibly on another node, possibly days later once a person answers its question — and what the rest of the turn needs is serialized into the pending-run row rather than parked in a goroutine. There is NO CONVERSATION in it (version 4): the run's own session holds its transcript, in its box, so what is kept is the turn's bookkeeping — the surface the run was offered, the rounds closed before it, what the turn was asked and by whom, what it wrote and owes — and the context the turn fetched once at its start, which every later launch of the turn is built from. NOT the notes the turn has read: those are on the run's record, read by the write that handed each over, which a resumed segment carries from (internal/engine, runsteer.go) — a second copy here could disagree with it. The sandbox coordinator carries it back without ever decoding it. Evolution is additive WITHIN a version and a reshape takes a new number, never a reused one. Once a TAG has shipped a version, the build that replaces it carries a reader for it for as long as rows of it can be parked: nothing rewrites a parked row, and the layer holding the blob cannot decode it. A version this build does not read — 1 to 3 included, which no tag ever shipped, 3 being the parked engine conversation images of main wrote and a number never reused — is refused loudly and the row left for a node that can |
| `agent/extension` | The round-cap extension judge: when a phase runs out of tool rounds, a cheap model says whether it is progressing or thrashing, and the engine grants more or falls through to the rescue path. THE ARITHMETIC IS SEPARATED FROM THE JUDGE — how many rounds a decision is worth is pure policy, impossible to exercise through a live model, so it is a value type tested directly. The judge reads the turn's ask with its fences MOVED off the turn's token onto one minted for its call (`Request.Boundary`, `provenance.Rekey`) beside the standing sentence, and the reason it gives is defused under the turn's token in the `Nudge` the seat reads, because a judge echoes what it read |
| `agent/subagent` | `delegate`: the short-lived WORKERS an executor hands work to. One tool loop with a persona (a `workers:` template or an inline prompt), a slice of the parent's own tools, a declared answer shape — the task's own `output` or its template's, the first held to the second's load-time rule (`config.ValidateWorkerOutput`) before anything runs — and hard caps on rounds, wall-clock and tokens. A worker is a LEAF — it delegates nothing further and contacts no colleague — which is the only thing bounding the depth of the construct, so there is no depth counter anywhere. One call is a GRAPH: `after` makes a task wait for others and receive their submitted fields, run in topological waves under one barrier each, because a wave boundary is checkable where a per-task readiness queue's deadlocks are not. Three rules are enforced in code because a prompt is only a request: the grant is a security boundary (a config template widens nothing — every name still passes the same filter), one budget slice covers a whole call however many tasks it has, and an absent submission is `no_result` with the worker's prose rather than an answer the engine invented Each worker is a tool loop like any phase — its model a harness running its own loop over the worker's slice of tools, served from the same tool-session host, which a config without one is refused for before any task runs — and a worker's model call on its parent's entry may run in the parent run's slot, when the entry has no free one, rather than wait for a slot only that run could free. |
| `agent/inbox` | What wakes a seat, and what it must not be woken by twice. `ledgered` is the completion side |
| `agent/ledger` | The prior-work ledgers: `iteration` (within a turn), `conversation` (across turns in one thread), `budgets`. Structured, never a transcript replay — the thread has moved by the next turn. A PAYLOAD past its budget — an argument value, a failed call's error, a round's produced text — is REWRITTEN by the seat's auxiliary model, never cut: the ledger only NAMES the pieces (`IterationPieces`, `SessionPieces`) and `ledgerfit` fits them, omitting an argument by size and digest where no rewrite can be had and carrying a draft whole. The cross-turn block keeps its newest entries whole and CONDENSES the older ones rather than dropping them |
| `agent/prefetch` | The turn-start context assembly: relevant knowledge, memory, the CHAT THREAD the turn was woken in, the blocks a prompt gets. FROZEN before the runner exists, which is what stops a self_iterate loop moving the system prompt underneath a turn. Nothing re-fetches mid-turn any more — the executor asks, with `search_knowledge`, over this same seam. The thread block is what a prompt telling an agent to go and read one cost: three MCP rounds before a word, and an agent that skipped them answering a pointer. It is bounded by ONE BYTE CEILING, anchored at a third of the conversation ledger's because both are frozen into one system prompt and re-sent every round: the root and the newest WHOLE messages are kept verbatim and the ones between them are CONDENSED by the seat's auxiliary model, dropped — and counted — only where no rewrite can be had; and its two failure sentences differ, because "nothing was said earlier" and "this node could not read it" send a seat to opposite places — the second covers a maintenance-mode node, an instance down at boot and a refused seat token, none of which is a chat API error. Its three AUXILIARY JUDGEMENTS (the memory filter, the knowledge-query writer, the episode summary) read the turn's ASK (`Request.Ask`, a part per trigger event, each a lead, its words and the `provenance.Label` they were read under — DATA rather than a rendering, so the engine carries the ask's merged label, `Ask.Label`, onto the turn's record of what it was asked) with somebody else's words FENCED as the trigger labels them, beside the standing sentence, under a token minted per call and never the turn's — what each answers decides what the seat is shown, and a model echoes the token it is handed — and the summary's briefing is defused under the turn's token before it enters the prompt; the vector and the recorded ask are the same text unfenced, since a random token in it would make every vector of one question differ (ADR-0040). Its block of SIMILAR PRIOR WORK renders each past turn through `learning.RenderPastTurn`, the one rendering `query_episodes` shares, so a past turn's ask is fenced under the label the episode kept — the turn's token for the block, the call's for the summary's input. EVERY BLOCK DEFUSES WHAT A STORE HANDS IT (memories, traits, skills, a past turn's account), because each is what a model wrote after reading other people's words, and a gate reads every string of `Blocks` by reflection rather than a list of blocks |
| `agent/prompts` | The prompt text, and one builder per phase — and EVERY BUILDER RETURNS ITS OUTLINE BESIDE ITS TEXT (`outline.go`): the parts it appended, each keyed, titled, measured and marked headed or not, because only the frame that appended a part knows where content quoting its own headings begins and ends. The engine STATES ONLY WHAT THE MODEL CANNOT KNOW, EACH RULE ONCE: the seat's identity and chart, the company's own words, the turn's prefetch and the phase's contract — a rule about a tool or a submission field lives in that tool's description or that field's schema, which reach the model with the tool on every round, and no tool the phase already holds is listed again (`## Tools to discover` names only what it can still reach). Prompt text is behaviour, changed deliberately with its tests and docs. Two prompts where there were three: the frame that DECIDES is the frame that acts, so the identity scaffold is sent once rather than twice and thrown away in between. The knowledge answer's system prompt (`KnowledgeAnswerSystem`) ends on `provenance.StandingInstruction` inside the constant itself, so its prefix stays byte-stable for a provider's cache, and `BuildKnowledgeAnswer` fences each source under the label the backend that found it gave it (`KnowledgeSource.Written`) rather than as one undifferentiated block (ADR-0040). A server-keyed tool skill fires on the servers the phase's registry holds a tool of (`Surface.MCPServers`, handed in by the runner from the snapshot the skill guard is armed against), never on the keys of the seat's `mcp_env`, which name credentials: a server turned off keeps its blocks and reaches nobody, and a shared server reaches every agent with no block at all. |
| `agent/skills` | Prompt fragments admitted from the knowledge base and injected per phase, plus the load-before-use guard. The registry is keyed by the PAGE a skill was read from, not by its key: every change names a page, and a page whose key was edited no longer says what it used to hold, so a key-addressed registry could never remove the old key. Pages as the identity are also what make a one-page update end exactly where a walk of the same pages would |
| `agent/skillsync` | The one loop per node that keeps the registry current: a walk when an apply moves the skills source (and the previous source retired at once), a read of the one page a webhook named, a `tool_skill_page_changed` broadcast so every node hears a webhook only one node consumed, a periodic walk behind all of it, and a bounded retry behind a walk that failed. ONE GOROUTINE DOES EVERY READ, which is what lets it need no version comparison: a later read always sees later state. Every change is READ BACK, a deletion included, because the backend and never the report says whether a page is gone. And a walk or a read that finds what the registry was built from changes nothing and logs nothing above debug, or the periodic walk would repeat every registry and audit warning on every node each interval |
| `agent/builtin` | The tools the engine itself ships, and the ONE AUTHORIZER that gates both catalogues (a seat's registry and the operator's), built from `authz.Decide` over the running chart and wrapped round every tool ONCE, at registration: the action IS the tool's name, so there is exactly one place the check could be forgotten. A NIL AUTHORIZER REFUSES, the opposite of a nil skill gate and deliberately so: a missing skill gate is a company that locked nothing, a missing authority decision is a surface that shipped without wiring one. What each tool is ABOUT is a table of ARGUMENT NAMES rather than closures, because a name can be walked against the tool's own schema and a closure can only be called with arguments somebody thought of; a personal verb that takes no handle is about the caller. The few checks that need a stored row first (re-routing a task, a project's lead-only facets, archiving it, setting somebody's priorities) ask the SAME seam from inside the tool under a dotted action, since which project a task is filed under is not in the arguments. A person verb that takes a name (`get_person`, `work_inbox`, `set_priorities`, and the HTTP surface's `MarkInboxFor`/`SetPinsFor`) resolves it to a RECORD first (`WorkDeps.personRecord`, over `iam.OwnerOf` and `WorkDeps.Holders`) and is decided on THAT, so it is `inTool`, but the directory is asked only once the verb has been decided on the record nobody has resolved yet (`WorkDeps.mayLook`, `authz.Object.Unresolved`), so a caller who leads nobody is refused before any lookup, in the very words a seat they do not lead gets, even when this node cannot read the directory; a login nobody holds is then decided on the name as typed before it is refused as naming nobody, and the directory's own words (which name the seat a login is bound to) go to the log and never into a refusal. Together that is what keeps the directory from being a roster. The same gate REFUSES AN ARGUMENT A TOOL DOES NOT DECLARE, naming it and what the tool reads, before any authority is asked (`ErrUndeclaredArgument`): a builtin reads its schema's properties and nothing else, so an undeclared one was DROPPED and the call answered as though it asked for less: the HTTP routes refused it and nothing else did, so a seat, a worker, a sandbox's bridge and the operator's assistant each saved `save_work_view`'s retired `owner` as a shared tab. The gate derives the principal FROM THE TURN when the context carries none, and never over an answer already there; and its wrappers are POINTERS, because a struct holding a func is uncomparable and the engine's epoch-identity `==` over a seat's tools panicked on it. THE ARTIFACTS TOOLS (artifacts*.go) are one per authority row, and each reads the stored artifact and asks `authz.DecideWithin` itself: for the caller, then for whoever the turn is acting for (the REQUESTER CEILING), sparing only edits to an agent's own work; every byte goes through internal/artifactupload, a coding run's box is listed and streamed through the box itself and never handed a URL, no tool takes `force` or shares an agent's work with the whole company, and a stale publish is answered with the live version, fenced. A FOLDER GRANTS NOTHING, so an agent files (`move_artifact`: editor on the artifact and contributor on the destination, both through the ceiling) and makes subfolders (`create_artifact_folder`, five records a segment) but never reorganises a tree; a folder reference resolves only among folders the agent AND the requester see, and a stranger's turn sees none. THE ADOPTED BRAND (`get_company_brand`) is everybody's under every ceiling, its voice fenced under the `company` trust, and a logo or face reaches a version only as a `from_brand` slot the pipeline copies. THE DATASTORE TOOLS (artifactdata*.go) take that same half for the head, the decision and the stamp and add only the datastore and a turn guardrail (1,000 ops a segment, a retry not charged twice): a row is answered only when the requester could read it too, and a write is stamped with the lower of the agent's role and the requester's. Both sets put a seat in the artifact's PRESENCE room, viewing on a read and editing on a write, once the call was allowed. CHATLET is the fourth domain here (`chat.go`, `chatoperator.go`): nine tools a seat holds and seven only a person makes, over ONE `ChatDeps` seam the estate router's `Chat`/`ChatSearch` satisfy (asserted at compile time). A room is said and READ as a seat, so an actor that names none is refused before any read; an edit's and a delete's authority is decided after reading WHOSE WORDS the message is (`remarkAuthority`: admin on the unread remark is MODERATION, carried to the store as `chatlet.MessageAuthority`), and an archive after reading the stored room (`chat.channel.archive`, its unit carried as a precondition). EVERY KEYED WRITE — a page's and a chat gesture's — takes its key from ONE derivation (`callOpKey`): in a turn the turn's seed, its instant, the tool, the arguments' digest and the repeat count among the tool's FAMILY (`chatlet.CallFamily`, so a join after a leave is a new join); outside one the key the transport carried; there is no per-domain copy. EVERY DOMAIN A SURFACE WRITES IS DECLARED ONCE (`nativeDomains`, writerclass.go): the refusal class and the sentence its writer's errors get and the arguments that name its objects, which the person's write surface reads for its tool-less verbs (`WriterClass`, `ObjectArgs`), held both ways to `estate.SurfaceWriteDomains` — so never the executor log, whose writes are the engine's own record of its runs and no surface's (the estate's engine-originated class), and whose writer no file here may name. WHICH OF ITS TOOLS HAND A MODEL SOMEBODY ELSE'S WORDS IS DERIVED, never listed: the plain encoder (`jsonAnswer`) refuses an answer holding a field a record declares somebody's words (`provenance.FieldTag`), a `foreignRead` refuses one it did not fence or name the writers of, and a sweep calls every tool both surfaces register against stores whose every declared field and every stored body spells a forged closer, holding each answer to the fence's property and the set of tools whose answers carry those words to `handsOverWords` in both directions — the tracker's listings, the inbox, the activity feed, a knowledge answer's sources and a parked run's question were each handed over bare before it. THE CATALOGUE (`catalogue.go`) files every built-in a seat is offered, and the runner's discovery pair, on one of eight GROUPS with the GATE its authority row asks (eleven words, from `everybody` to `artifact_owner`), because holding a tool is not being allowed to call it and a reader auditing what an agent holds needs both. The gate is DECLARED rather than read off `authz.ClassOf`, which is exported for walks and not for a caller to branch on: it names and never decides, and a walk holds every declared gate to its row's class, app and artifact role both ways (a personal verb that takes no name reads as its app, its object being the caller), so a row that changes turns the walk red until the catalogue agrees. `GET /tools`, the `tools` push and `mcp_servers_status`'s `builtin` shelves carry both, on the one row shape `queries.CatalogueRow` builds. |
| `agent/colleague` | Resolving a free-text query to a seat — the lookup before an A2A ask, a chat mention or a page mention. A model types what it remembers, so the answer is either one seat or an honest list of who it might be, NEVER a guess. Four tiers, earlier ones short-circuiting later, so a query that is exactly somebody's handle is never fuzzy-matched against everybody else |
| `agent/turnctx` | What a turn IS, carried as an explicit argument rather than ambient state. A turn has at least five values that want to travel ambiently, and a goroutine SHARES whatever context it captured — forever, including after the turn that created it finished — so what would elsewhere merely obscure a dependency is a live data race here. |
| `tools` | The registry, holding four kinds of tool under one contract and recording WHICH at registration: the only frame that knows, and the last that can say. A FIRST-PARTY refusal also carries its CLASS (`tools.Refusal`, defined in `mcp`) beside its sentence, AS ITS CAUSE: `Classify` states it beneath the error that decided it, `RefusalOf` reads it back and `UnknownOf` asks whether a write may have landed, with no class field beside the cause to tell one caller two stories, because the sentence is prompt text tuned for a model and a person's surface cannot parse it; an MCP server's failure stays unclassified, since the engine will not guess a class from prose it did not write. WHICH CLASS IS ONE RULE for every domain (the `Refusal` doc in `mcp`), by what the refusal is ABOUT and what would make the same request land: the request itself — an argument outside its grammar, a value past a limit of its own, an argument naming ANOTHER object that cannot take part (a parent in the trash) — is `invalid`; a COLLISION — a name, a title, a label, an address or an id the call gives an object that ANOTHER object already holds (a page title in its container, a room's name, a tag label another tag carries, a label another version holds, a source address another artifact holds), and a create of what is already there — is `exists`, its sentence naming the holder, since another value lands now and the same one once its holder gives it up; the object the call is about (or the container it writes into) absent is `not_found`; that object in a STATE that refuses the gesture until somebody changes it — a room or a project archived, an item frozen in the trash, an artifact in the trash, its own contents at their cap (a message's emoji, a project's tags, a task's watchers or checklists, a person's pins, every label an artifact keeps in use) — is `conflict`, as a lost race is; and a limit the vocabulary names a class for (`quota_exceeded`, `inbox_full`, `reassignment_budget`, `notes_full`, `notes_spent`, `budget_exhausted`) is that class. `agent/builtin`'s `TestEveryDomainRefusalIsClassed` holds every sentinel each native domain declares to the MEANING its row states and the class that meaning takes, on the domains' table and on each domain's own tools alike, and the meaning to the sentinel's own NAME where the name says one (a …Taken, …Clash or …Exists is a collision and every collision is named so; a …Full is the object's contents at their cap), so an archived room, an archived project and a trashed artifact cannot be three classes again, nor a collision be stated as a malformed request. `TestEveryFirstPartyRefusalIsClassified` walks the source for a failed result that forgot (the surface's own recorded `Call` and the loop's `ToolResult` included), because the surface refuses an unknown, unoffered or guarded call before any tool runs. A call that LANDED carries its caveats on `Result.Caveats` (`internal/caveat`) beside the model's words in its output, never rendered, for the surface that answers a person. Each MCP tool also carries what its server is FOR (`Entry.ServerDescription`), stamped at registration from what the registry was told (`DescribeServers`, from each apply's revision, carried by `Clone`) and kept ON THE ENTRY so every cut of a snapshot, a worker's grant rebuilt entry by entry included, keeps it; `Snapshot.Discoverable` prints it beside the server's name. |
| `tools/argval` | How a first-party tool READS ONE ARGUMENT a model sent: ONE READER PER DECLARED TYPE, which both decides whether a value is one and returns the value read (ADR-0059) — a string takes a number or a boolean as the model wrote it, a list of strings one string or a comma-separated one, an integer and a number a value or a decimal text that spells one exactly (`Whole`, `Number`), a boolean only true or false, and a closed set its member trimmed and without regard to case (`Choice`). The surface's check of a call against its schema calls it to decide and the builtin tools' helpers call the same reader to read, so a value the check admits is read as exactly what it spells: with a reader each, a set written as `"GP-12"` passed the check as a list and the tool read no list, and `"1e3"` passed as an integer and the tool read 1. A property test holds the check and every helper to the exact value over a corpus of awkward spellings. A LEAF over the standard library |
| `tools/toolargs` | A tool call's ARGUMENTS, from the transport that carried them to the one form every step of the call reads (ADR-0059). `Decode` is the ONE DECODE every transport into a tool shares — the HTTP write surface, the act transport, the operator's MCP and a harness's tool session: nothing at all (empty, whitespace or `null`) is a call with no arguments, and anything else is EXACTLY ONE JSON object, read THROUGH json.Number, so an integer past 2^53 reaches the tool as the digits it was sent as, and a second value after it is refused rather than dropped in silence. `Of` makes the CANONICAL FORM ONCE, AT THE ENTRY, a closed set's member as its set spells it (`" Draft"` is `"draft"`), and `Args`, a type only `Of` makes, is what every step after it requires: the run's call log and the repeat count an operation id carries, the skill guard, the refusal-loop target, the check, the tool, the ledger, logging and metrics. Made in the middle of that pipeline, the form let a retry spelt `"Draft"` after `"draft"` count as a different call before the tool and the same one inside it, so it took a fresh operation id and wrote twice. A LEAF BENEATH `tools`, not inside it, because the tool session sits below `tools` (the tools import the tool loop, which imports the session): kept beside the tools, the decode could not serve the session, which kept a copy of its own that took the first of two values. It imports `argval`, the standard library and `plainjson`, whose sentence says arguments that did not decode |
| `caveat` | What a write that LANDED tells its caller beside the outcome, in the words of each reader (ADR-0060). The builtin tools are shared by a seat's model and by a person in the dashboard, so a caveat carries a model's phrasing (it may name the tool and argument to use next) and a person's (only what a screen shows), written together by ONE constructor per code, and the action the code asks of a person (`required` stays on screen until dismissed, `none` goes by itself), stated per code and never guessed by a renderer. Its fields are unexported, so a caveat is made nowhere else, which is what makes the suite's walk over the catalog a walk over every caveat a person can be shown. A tool writes the model's words under its answer's one `warnings` key through `builtin.caveated` and hands the caveats to `mcp.Result.Caveats`; the act route answers `caveats` in the person's words and takes `warnings` off the receipt. NOT a code plus parameters worded by each surface: that is every sentence in two languages with nothing holding them to one set of codes. A REFUSAL has its person's half here too: a `Refusal` from the refusal catalog rides `mcp.Result.PersonRefusal` (lifted from a writer's error by `builtin.refusedBy` when the writer put it there with `Saying`), and `operator.Fail` takes that typed value and nothing else, so a refusal answers a person its catalog phrasing as `detail`, or no detail and its code's own sentence, never the model's. A refusal a configurable limit caused also carries a `Remedy` naming the `Setting` behind it (a token budget's: the company's, a seat's or each person's assistant's; or, for a model at a usage limit, the company's models), answered as `remedy`: a value the dashboard resolves to the screen that edits it and offers behind that editor's own gate, NOT an address or a list of grants, which would put the dashboard's routes and a second statement of who may write in the engine. `caveattest` holds the one check every walk over a person's sentence shares, and finds a model's framing (a fence's line, a defused marker, a model-only key) in any field of a person's answer, a value's included |
| `modelnote` | The sentence a tool call answered a MODEL with (a refusal's, or the note on a successful call's arguments, ADR-0059), carried IN PROCESS ONLY, from the route that answered it to an in-process caller whose own model made the request: the Dashboard Assistant executing a change its person approved (ADR-0060). A route answering a person answers a refusal in the person's words, or in its code's own sentence, and never the tool's, which names arguments and tools: right for the person, and wrong for the assistant's model, the one reader that can act on it, which handed only the floor could not tell its person what to change. So the route TELLS the tool's sentence here (`Tell`: the operator surface tells a refused call's beside its answer, and a successful call's note on its arguments), and only a caller that asked for it in its own request's context (`Expect`, the assistant's in-process dispatcher) hears it. NOTHING HERE IS EVER WRITTEN TO A RESPONSE: a request from a browser or a script carries no note, so a route telling one tells nobody, and the dispatcher reads the note off the context rather than off the answer. A LEAF over the standard library |
| `mcp` | MCP client and child supervision. A stdio server is a process TREE, not a process — and its environment is `hostbox`'s allowlist, the engine user's HOME and XDG directories, and what its `env:` and the seat's `mcp_env` declare, NEVER the engine's own: a server that reads anything else declares it, with a `${VAR}` reference to pass the host's value through, and lists the key under the server's `optional_keys` where a host may set none. A server whose `command` is `docker` or `podman` IS the runtime's CLI and gets `hostbox.ContainerRuntime`, the runtime's own settings beside the host user's locations, because a rootless runtime without its `DOCKER_HOST` dials the system socket. The package doc says why the obvious `os.Environ()` is wrong. Every server a bridge runs is SUPERVISED until it is stopped: the engine makes the child's one Wait itself rather than the SDK's transport, and watches the transport's read too, so a death — or a session that ended while its process lives on — is an event and not a broken pipe found by the next call. The server is restarted on ONE schedule (`RestartWait`: doubling and jittered from one seat heartbeat to the undead alarm interval, at once after a healthy run), the same one a failed start is retried on whoever retries it, its tools staying listed and refusing `unavailable` meanwhile through the SAME Tool objects, which reach their server rather than one session. Whether this node may start a per-role child on its own is the owner's call (`Supervision.MayStart`), because the child is a seat's identity; a shared server's failed start is retried by its bridge, since nothing else would ask again, and a seat's by the seat's runner, so the bridge keeps nothing of it A TOOL CARRIES ITS DEFINITION'S DIGEST (`digest.go`): the server's own name, title, description, both schemas and declared hints, never the prefix or an operator's override, so a company's edit never reads as the server changing a tool. `RiskOf` is the ONE reading of a tool's hints as a risk (an undeclared tool is destructive, and says no hint decided it), and `Bridge.Identity` what a server said of itself in its last handshake. |
| `mcp/catalogue` | The MCP servers this build KNOWS: the curated table Settings › MCP & tools adds a server from (`Curated`), ONE Go table reviewed in pull requests, so every node answers it the same with or without egress, each entry recording the day its facts were read from the vendor (`ReadAt`) and a launch through a package runner the exact release it fetches (`Package`, `Release`), which `config`'s toolservers test holds every page that launches the same package to. The ONE RECOGNITION of a configured server by its address or its command (`Recognise`), read three ways: the status answer's `title`, `catalogue` and `vendor`, a community listing's vendor mark, and the seat credential `config` binds to a per-seat server it recognises (`SeatKeysFor`, `BindingFor`), which is why an address is matched by whole host labels and never a substring. And the search of a COMMUNITY REGISTRY a company names (`mcp_registry`, the MCP Registry v0.1 API, `Registry.Search`), which is untrusted remote content: https only (`CheckRegistryAddress`), bounded in bytes, listings and time, every field validated, a listing it cannot offer whole left out and counted, no redirect followed and nothing a listing names ever dialled; a result is a draft marked community, never an integration, a fixed value or a vendor mark it did not earn by its address |
| `a2a` | Agent-to-agent asking: one ask, one answer, then closed. THE CHANNEL IS AN AUTHORIZATION RECORD, NOT A TRANSPORT — nothing queues here, and both the brief and the reply travel over the durable seat inbox, so a colleague owned by another node is an ordinary target. The in-process bus this replaced woke the target on whichever node owned ITS seat, where the queue was empty. Two capabilities over one store, publisher and clock (`keeper`): the `Service` ADDRESSES (and refuses to be built without a `Directory`), the `Sweeper` addresses nobody and closes an idle channel only once the engine says its answer is NO LONGER OWED (`Owed`, which it refuses to be built without, three-valued, an unknown spared for the tick): a coding run has no clock bound and holds the ask on its record, or holds its seat's inbox with the ask waiting in it, so a channel closed on idleness alone refused the answer that came back and left the asker, whose turn ended when it asked, never woken |
| `decision` | The shape of a structured ask, shared by everywhere an agent asks a person to choose (ADR-0023, ADR-0068): `Decision` (one-sentence question, two to six options with slug ids, the one recommended and why, evidence, role, and an `inform` only a tracker ask may carry), its caps and `Decision.Validate`, every fault at once. VALUES ONLY: a work item's decision is checked against the world by `worklet.ValidateDecision`, and a coding run's ask (`sandbox.Ask`) adds its context and branch; both refuse in this package's words. HERE AND NOT IN `worklet`, because `worklet` already depends on `sandbox`, so a decision declared in the tracker could never ride a run's row; a second copy of the shape in `sandbox` would be two contracts a model meets and a person reads, free to drift. |
| `worklet` | Worklet, the engine's own work tracker, as `statelog`'s FIRST DOMAIN: its records, its subjects and the arithmetic that has no database in it. Every change is one record published to the subject of the object it changes, so THE SUBJECT IS THE ARBITRATION UNIT rather than a routing label: two writers racing on one task contend at the broker and exactly one wins, and two writers on different tasks never contend at all. The record decodes in TWO PASSES — an envelope every build reads and a version-gated payload — which is what lets a node file a newer peer's record under its subject, probe for it, drop it through a gate and reprocess it later; a record that failed to decode at all yields no id, no kind and no subject to file it under. Its `Scope` is the complete set of objects an apply may write, stated by the WRITER, and the paths NEST — an object's path is written under its container — because that is the only shape under which a record deferred on a project blocks a write to a task in it while a record deferred on one task does not block its neighbour. `rank.go` is the manual order as a VALUE TYPE: a byte-comparable key with a magnitude head that borrows and carries, so repeated head insertion grows the key by about a character per sixty-two placements rather than exhausting a thirty-symbol alphabet, and the two mint sets — a create's pure integer at or above the origin, and every `rankorder` mint below it or carrying a fraction — are disjoint BY THE SHAPE OF THE VALUE, so the proof mentions no counter, no staleness and no in-flight create. A DEPENDENCY is the one relation with two ends and therefore the one gesture that is a SEQUENCE — `waiting_on` AUTHORED on the dependent, `Dependents` MIRRORED on the blocker — and the asymmetry decides everything: the counterparties are read before the first append so a full, tombstoned or absent blocker refuses the edge; the authored commit lands; the mirror is BEST EFFORT, because the edge is durable without it and refusing the whole gesture over it would lose what somebody asked for. The residue is a ONE-SIDED edge, which the applier DERIVES rather than the record carries — whether a blocker lists this task is a fact about the other end's rows, so a writer stating it would be stating what it read in another transaction on another subject, which is why the flag had no producer and the partial index the schema ships for the repair duty was over a column that was zero on every row; since replicated 0061 that index holds both ends, so the repair walks the waiting edges in the order it selects them and stops after a batch rather than sorting every one. `one_sided_final` is the opposite kind of fact and stays on the record: the duty's DECISION that this edge will never be mirrored. Every collection a record carries whole takes a GESTURE resolved inside the decide — `Watch`, `Relate`, `Depend`, `Checklist` — for one reason each time: a caller cannot form a set it read in another transaction, and a cap governs the set the write LANDS on. Every refusal the writer writes is MARKED and what is unmarked is the node's: `ErrInvalid` for what was asked (a parent in the trash, an archived type, a value past its cap), `ErrState` for what the item or project the write is about refuses by being what it is now (frozen in the trash, archived, its tags, pins, watchers or checklists at their cap), `ErrTaken` for a COLLISION, a value the write would give an object that another already holds (a tag's label another tag carries, an id a checklist or a line already has), `ErrForbidden` for who asked — so a caller is told to change the request only when the request is what is wrong. And the routing precedence is ORDER IN ONE FUNCTION rather than a list beside it: what THIS COMMIT did to you outranks the role you hold, which is what `mention`, `asked`, `answered` and `blocking` sit above `assignee` for — behind it `blocking` could name nobody at all, since the only handle it ever adds is the blocker's own assignee. `coerce.go` is the CUSTOM-FIELD table and it is PURE over values, for the reason `textindex`'s arithmetic is: a rule exercised only through a database is a rule nobody re-reads. It runs at the WRITE because that is the only place a value can be REFUSED — the applier deliberately salvages, since refusing a malformed value there would stop that task's every later change on every data node — and it NORMALISES what it accepts, so the record carries one canonical form and every data node writes identical rows without re-deciding. Nothing is rounded to fit: a field exact to two places refuses `3.14159` rather than storing a number nobody typed. The one seam it declares is the ROSTER, because a people field's value belongs to the EPOCH rather than to a row; a relationship's task is deliberately not a seam, being a row this package reads inside the decide's own transaction. Its AUTHORITY VALUES — `ProjectAuthority`, `TagAuthority`, `PersonAuthority` — state the TABLE'S DECISION and never its inputs: they used to carry a lead flag and an "is an operator" flag that every caller combined for itself, which was a second copy of the rule that consulted no permission, so an administrator was refused here while every HTTP route allowed. The caller asks `internal/authz` and passes what came back; this package keeps only the half a chart cannot know — which FACET each answer unlocks — and the one rule of its own, which is about the gesture rather than anybody's authority: an AGENT never re-orders a colleague's queue, even one it leads, because that is a hand-off in disguise that bypasses the guarded take and the reassignment budget. EVERY STATEMENT THAT READS A TASK TABLE IS NAMED AND PLANNED: the index gate (`TestEveryIndexServesARegisteredQuery`) holds each registered statement to what it is — a seek of the index it names, a walk with no sort, a census with no heap scan, and no read, census or history read planned as answering a per-row aggregate from its whole child table (`storetest.Decorrelated`; the passes excepted, a grouped read of all of it being what a pass is) — and its completeness walk reads every non-test file of the MODULE, refusing a literal that reads a task's table — its relations and its dependency mirror (`tracker_relations`, `tracker_task_dependents`) among them — and belongs to no registered statement, and refusing to pass having read nothing outside this package (the applier's relation statements are named in `apply_relations.go`, where the store's exclusivity gate admits a tracker write, and the one-sided repair's probe in `onesided.go`), because another package's reads (search's walk, fetch, count and backlinks, which it names as exported statements for this gate to plan) were planned by nobody while one of them sorted every live task for each batch. A REMARK IS APPLIED BY KIND: a post is an insert of a row that is not there yet, closing the ask it answers only if it wrote that row, and a re-run of the post is answered from that row by its decide with nothing published, no history row, wake or watch, and resolved from it by the thread read before anything is inferred (`Reader.Thread`, the read `AskedOn` asks too), so the re-run reports, and is admitted for, the question its post answered rather than whatever is open to its author now (`WriteResult.Posted` is the decide's own account of that row); an edit rewrites the words alone and a resolve writes the resolution alone (`resolved`, `resolved_by`, `resolved_at`) on a row the node holds, each reaching the applier only through its own gesture (`EditComment`, `ResolveComment`), which forms it from the stored row and refuses anybody but the author — because one upsert of everything a record carried wrote NULL over `answered_by`, the ANSWERING remark's column, which no record carries, so an author rewording a question somebody had answered reopened it on every board and in every count of open asks; no gesture takes a tracker comment down yet, so `removed` is written by nothing and a record of the removal kind writes nothing, and `UpdateTask` refuses a comment under that kind. And `answered_by` is DERIVED, the fifth column `Rederive` brings to this build's rule (`rederiveAnswers`: the first answer the log carries after its question, each remark placed by the history row its posting record wrote, which `record_id` names), because a node holding rows another rule set wrote would otherwise keep that rule's stamps on a table the fleet compares byte for byte. Its applier also says WHOSE INBOX a committed batch moved: one `InboxMovement` per recipient per batch — identifiers and a count, never content, and the broker's instant for the OLDEST record that moved it (`At`, which crosses the live relay with the rest and which the socket's push is timed from: change visibility) — announced POST-COMMIT, because the transaction body can re-run and a movement announced inside it could describe a write that rolled back, and noted only when the notice row was actually written, so a redelivery the history guard already absorbed announces nothing. The count is a HINT, keyed on (record, recipient). A REMOVAL IS REVERSIBLE AT ANY AGE, which is the work tracker's promise, so the tracker hands the trash reaper no source and its one destructive step stays a purge somebody asks for. Its read types DECLARE the fields that hold somebody else's words (`untrusted:"fence"` or `untrusted:"name"`, `provenance.FieldTag`), which the agent's read tools may not answer unlabelled |
| `changefeed` | How a committed record becomes a WAKE: a durable, fleet-wide pull consumer over one ESTATE — a state machine's log. The estate is a SEAM rather than a document family (a coordination type that left with the last projector), because a log delivery has no family, no key class and no change record, and manufacturing a family for one would have started a projector over a bucket that does not exist: a `Source` names it as a string, an `Opener` opens one consumer over it, and a `Record` is one delivery from it. The principle is that a wake is derived by something that OUTLIVES the writer, never published by the writer's goroutine as a courtesy — the chat path already swallows a failed publish and the webhook path is safe only because the vendor retries, so the durable record is the retry. A group rather than a duty, because a lease flap on a duty holder would stall the whole company's notifications for stateless work. TWO dedupe layers, each covering the other's gap: a claim collapses a redelivered message and FAILS OPEN (a coordination store that cannot be reached must not silently stop notifications), and a deterministic per-recipient wake id lets the inbox and the completion ledger collapse what the open claim let through. A first-party wake also carries the COMMITTING RECORD'S POSITION (`trigger_position`), and the turn it starts observes it before its first read, so a woken seat never reads the change that woke it as not there yet — whichever holder answers. Every published wake is recorded as `wake_latency`, timed from the record's broker instant (`Record.StoredAt`) to the publish. A FEED OUTLIVES ITS CONSUMER: `Run` returns only when its context ends, and a consumer that fails to open, fails a read (a missed broker heartbeat after a host sleeps) or closes under a live context is stopped and its durable group opened again on a doubling, jittered wait from one second to `statelog.FeedReopenCeiling` (half the stall grace), the series beginning again only after a consumer that stayed open for the ceiling. The loop is here rather than in the engine because the failure is the consumer's and every caller has it; before it, one heartbeat error ended a node's feeds for good. A feed with no consumer publishes no wake, so `wake_latency` cannot see it: `Feed.Outage` says since when and why, and the engine reads it into `feed_stopped`. A translator may hold this node's DECISIONS (chat's `NodeDecisions`, over the replicated estate and the domain runner the engine's feed hands it) and withhold the wake of a record its own applier refused, FAILING OPEN wherever none is decided yet, because the record carries its own routing snapshot so the company's wakes never wait on one node's applier |
| `doclet` | Doclet, the engine's own KNOWLEDGE BASE (containers, pages, revisions and the change record), and `statelog`'s THIRD domain, its second STRICT one, which is what tells a per-domain checkpoint from a per-node one. There is no projection and no coordination family behind it any more: a write is one record on `CREWLET_PAGES_LOG`, an applier writes every data node's rows, and a read reports the POSITION it answered at rather than a hydration boolean. Adopting the log removed all three of the two-key sequences the bucket forced: a create is ONE record whose apply writes the title, the head, the first revision and the history entry together, so there is no orphan claim and no grace rule for stepping over one. THE SUBJECT IS THE ADDRESS — a create arbitrates on the TITLE rather than on the new page's uuid, because two writers must contend for a name and two uuids never would — and the title travels as a bounded TOKEN, since a subject is a broker path and a title is prose carrying spaces, dots and wildcards; the applier recomputes it from the payload and REFUSES a record that took one address and claimed another. Plus the three things a wiki has that a tracker does not. A TITLE IS AN ADDRESS, so it is unique per container and it is what a create ARBITRATES ON. A BODY HAS A HISTORY, so revision N is the body at version N, including the newest — the other reading, that a revision holds what was replaced, collides with itself on the first save and leaves the current body reachable only through the head; a save must state the version it edited, unlike a work item's optional If-Match, because there is no per-field merge that makes overwriting prose safe. And a page is SEARCHED rather than filtered, so `Searcher` fulfils the `knowledge` seam and Doclet is a drop-in for Confluence. A RENAME IS ITS OWN OPERATION rather than a field of a save, because one record has one subject and a record carrying both an address change and a content change could arbitrate only one of them; it stamps the head's `scoped_through` and never its `version`, so the page's own expectation is never poisoned. A COMMENT DOES NOT SUBSCRIBE ITS COMMENTER here, which is the opposite of the tracker's participants rule and deliberate: a page a hundred people have remarked on would otherwise wake a hundred seats when somebody fixes a heading. The applier is also what NOTICES a tool-skill page moving — natively there is no page webhook and the change feed deliberately drops those changes, so the apply, which already derives the skill flag, is the only thing that sees both an arrival and a departure. THE RESERVED CONTAINERS ARE THE STORE'S RULE, not a tool's: it was one check on `write_page`, so a seat that could not create a page in the skills container could still save over one, rename one and comment on one, and every surface added later would have inherited the hole silently — now every write path asks, reading the keys live because they are Tier B. The two containers are reserved for DIFFERENT REASONS and refuse different gestures, which is `Reserved`'s shape: the SKILLS container is machinery excluded from search and routing, so an AGENT is refused every write there — create, save, rename and every comment path; the ORG ROOT is the company's canon and is searched and routed like any other, so an agent is refused only ADDING a page. Folded into one list, every agent write into the root was refused with a sentence claiming its pages were unreadable. It is a rule about the ACTOR and not about capability: a seat cannot see that a page there is machinery, and a person publishing there on purpose is what those containers are for. The CAPABILITY half is the tool layer's, because the store exempts every person and holds no permissions: a person's create, save, rename, trash, restore or purge of a page in the SKILLS container is asked `pages.skill.write` (`admin`, never a seat) once the container is known — a tool skill is injected into every seat's turn, so the `knowledge` app alone had been a way to rewrite the prompt the whole company runs under. A PAGE IS CREATED ONLY IN A DECLARED SPACE (`Reserved.Declared`: every unit's and seat's `space:`, `knowledge.root_space` and the skills container, from `engine.reservedContainers` over `chartContainers`), refused for a person and an agent alike by `refuseUndeclared` — a separate check with neither of `refuseReserved`'s early returns, so a store with no `Reserved` seam declares nothing and creates nothing; only `Create` asks it, a page's container never moves after. A REMARK IS ITS AUTHOR'S: an edit is refused to anybody else in the decide's own snapshot, and a removal — which had no check at all — takes the author or a caller the table admits as a moderator, passed as `CommentAuthority` because this package holds no chart and no grants. A LISTING IS READ IN ITS ORDER: replicated 0062 keys the heads by container, title and id, the order every listing is drawn in, and a later window resumes it through `keyset`'s arms, so a large space's tenth window costs what its first does — with no index in that order every window read and sorted every page its listing matched, and resumed through one disjunction no index could seek. A page's history is read newest first IN LOG ORDER (replicated 0063, `pages_history_page_idx` on the page and its position), so a listing row's last save and one page's own change feed stop early rather than sorting a history saved thousands of times. Replicated 0064 dropped the three page indexes no reader's plan reached (the history's by creation instant, the revisions' second copy of their own key, the skills' by container) and with the last of them `pages_skills.container`, a copy of the head's container that only that index read — an index nothing reads being a write on every apply on every data node, carried in every snapshot; the trash's stays, because the trash reaper's listing seeks it, and since replicated 0066 it is on the trash instant and the page's id, the listing's own order, so that listing stops at its cap where it sorted every page past the cutoff. THE NEWEST TOOL SKILLS ARE THE OTHER ORDER (`OrderNewest`): the skill flag is a DIVERGENT row of its own and not a column of the head, so since replicated 0067 that row carries its head's `updated_at` (the column was `at`, which nothing read) and `pages_skills_newest_idx`, partial on the flag, holds the skills newest first — a window walks it and reads each head by its key, across a CROSS JOIN because joined plainly the planner drove on the heads and sorted — and the order is served beside `skills=true` alone (`ErrUnservedOrder`), its cursor naming the order it is a place in. EVERY STATEMENT THAT READS A PAGE TABLE IS NAMED AND PLANNED, as the tracker's are (`TestEveryIndexServesARegisteredQuery`): a point read, probe or batch read held to a seek of the index it names and the selection to a walk of its index with no sort, a census to no heap scan, no read or census answering a per-row aggregate from its whole child table (`storetest.Decorrelated`), the passes to no sort; its completeness walk refuses a literal in the module's non-test source that reads a covered table and belongs to no registered statement, and its inventory an index the schema declares on one that no registered plan reaches — which is how the three 0064 dropped were found. And `ContainerKey` is the one canonical form of a container key, because eleven copies had drifted and six of them did not trim. The knowledge base's TOOL NAMES are this domain's (`tools.go`), aliased by `agent/builtin` as the tracker's and chat's are, so a rename lands in one place. Its read types DECLARE the fields that hold somebody else's words (`untrusted:"fence"` or `untrusted:"name"`, `provenance.FieldTag`), which the agent's read tools may not answer unlabelled |
| `iamdomain` | The company's own IDENTITY ESTATE: people, the addresses and logins they are known by, the credentials they hold, the invitations that enrolled them and the sessions they are signed in with, as `statelog`'s FOURTH domain and its third STRICT one. `iam` stays the VALUES LEAF; this is the durable half and it imports that leaf rather than restating it. What is particular to it is that ARBITRATION IS THE CONSTRAINT: the replicated estate forbids a UNIQUE index outside a primary key, a violation inside an apply transaction aborts it deterministically on EVERY data node, which here is every login in the company, so there is no uniqueness check anywhere in the schema and nothing to add one to. What keeps two people off one address is ONE SUBJECT FOR THE DIRECTORY (`iam.directory`): every record that sets or frees an address, a login or a seat, an enrolment (an administrator's `Create` or an invitation's `Redeem`, two verbs over one `OpEnrol` record and one decide), an invitation's issue and its cancellation, an identity change (`SetIdentity`, the login and the seat as post-state), a removal, publishes there, decided in a snapshot of EVERY row, so a value somebody else holds is refused naming them (`ErrTaken`, an open invitation by its id) and the broker accepts the record only if no other directory record landed since. It was a subject PER CLAIM, so an enrolment was a sequence whose half-finished residue was a reservation every reader stepped around, a move was a claim and a release, and a duty reported duplicates nothing could refuse. THE DIRECTORY OWNS A ROW'S EXISTENCE AND ITS THREE UNIQUE COLUMNS, an enrolment creates the row whole, a removal deletes it, and a record on the person's own subject (`iam.person.<id>`) only ever UPDATES a row that exists, so the two subjects meet on one row and never on one column, and one `version` guards both. A DIRECTORY DECISION ASKS THE WHOLE DEFERRAL INDEX (`wholeDirectory`), because the framework's probe covers the buckets a record declares, the people it writes, never the people it compares against, so a node holding any record it cannot decode refuses `deferred` and another node decides; a removal frees values and does not ask. TWO ROWS HOLDING ONE VALUE, a retained record's residue, or a restore's, answer a lookup UNKNOWN rather than either row.
