| `assistant` | The Dashboard Assistant's ORCHESTRATOR: a person's question answered by a model through `agent/toolloop`, with a Surface of its own over the curated catalogue, every read made as that person and every write prepared as a proposal they approve. NOT A SEAT, so none of the agent runtime's turn machinery is used: no lease, no roster entry, no diary, no work item to charge. It holds an `exec.Grant` for at most one turn, a handle it can name and time but never present, and a parked turn holds none (ADR-0050). An IMPORT WALK (`TestTheAssistantImportsNoWriterAndNoSeat`) fails the build on any package under it importing the store, the state-log framework or the fleet's secrets, or reaching at any depth the operator surface, the question registry, the tool registry, the builtins, the MCP bridge (which acts as a seat), the agent turn context (which falls back to a seat's principal), the credential owner (`api/auth`), another domain's writer or reader, or the engine. `Service` STARTS a turn only once the person may use it (`iam.Assistable`, the predicate the guard derives grants by), their load allows another (two running, twenty a minute), the company's windows have room (`toolloop.Refusal` on the turn's meter, before any round) and the chain resolves; the SAME KEY IS THE SAME TURN (`TurnID`, derived from the request's scoped `Idempotency-Key`), and a grant no runner takes is revoked on the way out. A FAILURE OF ITS OWN RECORDS, ITS COUNTER OR ITS AUTHORITY TABLE is that store's own error, which the HTTP surface tells as a condition or a fault, and a write whose outcome is unknown is returned as one; `Unavailable` is kept for its own reasons (no model chain it can build, a node shutting down, a runner it cannot reach) and carries no wait. The PROMPT is built in cache order (`prompt.go`: rules stamped `PromptVersion`, `provenance.StandingInstruction`, the company clock, the person and the writes they may propose, the last twelve messages with cards as one-line summaries, then the question after the page context FENCED as `page_context`), and the SURFACE refuses an identity argument or one its schema does not declare before anything is sent, holds every request to a route its tool declares, sends only through the dispatcher on the turn's read grant (`TestTheAssistantSendsThroughTheDispatcherAlone`), redacts an answer before it is carded, and fences and cuts what the model reads to 16 KiB; a write offers itself only where a `Proposer` is wired, and a 401 on the turn's own grant ends it `grant_ended`. A segment ends parked, completed, stopped, failed or out of budget, recorded through the consumer-defined `Records` (the engine adapts the domain's writer, deriving every operation from the gesture's key), streamed through `live`, and audited as `assistant_turn_completed`. It IS the approval path's `approval.Turns`: `Resume` takes a parked loop back up on the deciding request's own grant, answering the call it suspended on with what became of the proposal (the engine's words, the route's answer fenced as an API answer, a page's report fenced as the page's), each of which taints the resumed segment as a step's read does, beside the taint the park kept (D25), and updating the proposal card in place by its first card id; `Emit` streams a page action to a turn that stays parked; a Stop of a parked turn declines what it waits on through `approval.DeclineTurn` (bound with `BindDecliner`, since the approver is built over the service), under the Stop's own key; and `Render` is the compliance read's rendering, the owner's own rule. Its answer STREAMS: every round is asked to stream, its settled lines are drawn as written, and each change is one `llm.Reconcile` of the drawn text against what the drawer should hold, a restart where the drawn text is no prefix of it. ACROSS A FLEET (`fleet.go`) it is the running node's half of `live`'s channels (`ReplayHere`, `StopHere`, each refusing a person the turn is not for), `FleetRemote` hands it the fleet half with another person's turn answered not found, a stream of a turn whose runner is gone (its node says it is not running it, or has left the roster) closes it interrupted under an operation every node derives alike, so of two nodes noticing one records it, and a person's turn starts are counted on the fleet's counter (`TurnRate`, twenty a minute) beside the node's own rate, which is what holds them while the counter cannot be read. Stop decides from the turn's record before a local runner, since a runner that just parked is still tearing down. A segment is numbered after the newest seq the fleet holds and never below the parked record's (`continuing`), and a stream is answered by the node holding the frames its client has not drawn (`streamSource`). A turn's END IS SETTLED, never only logged (`settle.go`): the runner retries its close under the same operation until it lands, the turn is found already ended or its conversation is gone, within the frame log's retention, and an interrupted close that did not land is the stream's error rather than an `interrupted` the record does not say; everything a resume can fail on is read before its resume record lands. A turn may hand its task to DEEP WORK (`start_deep_work`, deep.go): a harness with its own harness whose one way into Crewlet is this turn's own surface, bridged; a proposal parks the turn and its approval continues the run in the same box (ADR-0054), and a file the run delivers that the store did not keep for a condition of the node comes back `Unkept`, worded for a model by the engine (which reaches the condition table this package may not), so the run's note says what kept it out |
| `assistant/wire` | The assistant's PROTOCOL VOCABULARY, a leaf over the standard library, uuid, `caveat` (the `Remedy` an error frame carries) and `words` (itself a leaf, through which a refusal names the closed set a value comes from): the ten event kinds, every closed set as a named string type with `Valid()`, every frame, card, page context and route body, and the stream's one encoding (`Encode`, `Decoder`, `EventID` as `<turn_id>.<seq>`, `KeepaliveComment`). A frame NESTS its payload under a member named for its kind, so a head member a later version adds can never collide with a payload's and the client narrows a frame by its kind alone. `dashboard/src/contract/assistant.ts` is held to it BOTH WAYS by three clientsource gates (every closed set, every limit, and every shape's members with their optionality, an `omitempty` field being an optional member) and a walk that fails on a JSON-tagged struct no gate holds; the dashboard's own suite replays `testdata/frames.sse`, which this package's encoder writes |
