A LEAF the credential owner imports rather than the other way round: `api/auth` implements `Dispatcher` and mints the only `Grant`, so the orchestrator never holds the grant's token, and the in-process marker the guard requires is an unexported key there that nothing here can set |
| `assistant/cards` | Tool answers to ENGINE-BUILT CARDS (table, entity, open in app, error, notice), from the route's own JSON by the tool's descriptor and never by the model, capped at 1000 rows and 256 KiB with a notice when cut. An answer no descriptor was written for is inferred (`Infer`) by ONE RULE: a list, or a record wrapping one list beside plain values and no field it is known by (a page of a collection), is a table; any other record is ONE THING, an entity of its facts and every list of records it holds, which a table of its largest list misdrew; such an entity names no dashboard `kind`. A field that is BOOKKEEPING (`Bookkeeping`: an epoch, a digest, a cursor, and the state log's envelope on every read it serves, its read level, completeness, lag and log position, a nested field beneath one included) stays for the model and the downloads and is left out of the drawer. An open in app link is the engine's, from a link table held against the dashboard's routes, and its `permitted` is asked for the person by sending the record's own read through their grant. A RECORD IS NEVER NAMED BY A VALUE NOBODY READS: a link named by nothing is named by what it opens, by a noun where its address takes a UUID or a handle ("Open room") and by the key where it takes one a person types, held over the whole link table by a walk derived from it, and an entity known only by its UUID is headed by its spec's name, never by the id, and a direct conversation by its people beside the reader as the turn says the room (`Entity.Room`, `Options.Room`), so two read alike are still two; a room is drawn after the "#" every screen draws before its name (`Column.Prefix`, `Entity.TitlePrefix`), never before nothing, and keyed by it (`Entity.KeyPrefix`); a thread is headed by its first words, cut where they pass a heading (`Entity.TitleCut`). A stored card is shown again only while the person holds every app it was built from (`Visible`), and the link table is replayed through the dashboard's router from a golden file. A REFUSAL A PERSON READS IS THE ENGINE'S SENTENCE (`PersonRefusal`), at every exit to a person (a step's line, a read's error card, a failed proposal's strip): where the authority table refused, the envelope's own sentence for the rule that refused (`httpjson.RefusalMessage`), with its `reason` kept beside the grants, since the grants alone cannot say whether access is the way past; where the route answered the refusal in a person's words (a `detail` the route marked by telling the tool's own sentence beside it in process, `wire.Refusal.Said`), those words, redacted and held to the rule every sentence keeps (`ForAPerson`); any other refusal, one chosen by its code (a 409 or a 412 is a race only for the codes that are one, and each state and collision has a sentence of its own), else its status. An unmarked `detail`, which names tools, fields and config keys, rides `wire.Refusal.Detail`, never serialised, to the model alone. A 409 is worded as a collision only when it is one: a turn's notes full or spent, a turn that ended, an item handed on as often as it may be, a full inbox, a spent budget and an answered question each read as what they are, and a failed proposal no change to the same request can mend is hinted `RefusalSettled`, never promised a change that can apply (`RefusalHint`) |
| `assistant/proposal` | A proposal's PURE LOGIC (ADR-0051): the digest binding an approval to the request a server-side preview showed (owner, conversation, turn, scope, tool, verb, step-up, the bound request in `api/opkey`'s canonical encoding, source revision, expiry), the legal moves between `wire.ProposalState`s (`Next`, one table), expiry and the lazily decided unknown (`StateNow`), and the card's rows in decision D10's grammar (fact, changed, added, removed, a long text as a word diff) with D09's audience, drawn by `New` from the descriptor's own `Preview` and never from model text. No I/O. Unknown is not settled: a Retry sends the same operation |
| `assistant/proposal/approval` | Carrying a person's decision out (ADR-0051): `Approve`, `Decline`, `DeclineTurn` (Stop's hook) and a page's claim and report of a page action. Decided for the principal the request resolved, never an owner it is handed, so an approval is never another person's; the digest echoed and re-hashed, a step-up asked BEFORE anything is recorded, the revision re-read as the person, then one ARBITRATED approval and one write sent in process under a single-use exec grant with the proposal's own operation key, so two tabs on two nodes send one write and a Retry after unknown is the same write. Under `internal/assistant`, so the import walk keeps it off `api/auth`: grants, turns and the store are seams. A RECORD THE STORE REFUSED WROTE NOTHING, and a read of its records that failed came before anything was decided, so each is the store's own error, which the surface tells as a condition or a fault; `Unconfirmed` is kept for a record sent and never answered, or one stating what already happened (a sent write's settlement) |
| `assistant/live` | A turn's LIVE STREAM on its running node: a bounded per-turn frame log in memory (2000 frames or 4 MiB), never stored (ADR-0038's rule for a stream), local subscribers disconnected rather than dropped from past 512 buffered frames, and the FLEET HALF (`Fleet`): every frame a node appends is broadcast on the turn's own ephemeral subject ENCRYPTED under the fleet keyring's AEAD (the `secrets` cipher the conversation is sealed with at rest) with the turn in its associated data, inside a signed one-way seal in key domain `assistant.frame`, so reaching the broker reads no conversation and a frame the fleet did not seal, or captured off another turn, is dropped; a node streaming a turn it does not run listens first and fills every gap, and a quiet stream's possibly lost tail, by asking the running node on the signed `assistant.replay` channel (its pages encrypted the same way), and a Stop reaches the runner on `assistant.stop`, both answered only by the node the request names, which checks the person (`not_yours`). A request nobody answers is a runner GONE only when the roster says its node left the fleet; a roster that cannot be read is never gone. ONE NUMBERING ACROSS THE FLEET: a PROBE on `assistant.replay`, which every node answers from its hub (`Hub.Known`), tells a node opening a turn's next segment (a resume or a page action an approval emitted on another node) the newest seq any node numbered, and `Hub.Open` numbers after it, so a client that drew frames from one node never meets a number it has seen; the same probe names the node holding a turn's newest frames, where a stream of it is followed A frame the protocol refuses takes NO SEQ, so a client never reads a hole for a frame that was never sent; the `Hub` keeps an ended segment's frames two minutes for a reconnect and a turn's last seq for as long as a turn may wait on an approval, so a resumed segment continues the numbering its first one drew |
| `assistant/assistanttest` | The assistant's TEST support, TEST-ONLY: `Golden` (compare a committed fixture, or rewrite it under `CREWLET_REGENERATE_ASSISTANT_FIXTURES=1`, which `make assistant-fixtures` sets), `Turn`, which numbers a turn's frames as a running node does, `ExampleArgs`, `World` and `ProposalArgs` (the company every write previews against), and `NewRig`, which wraps `assistant/evals`' assembly with a `*testing.T` and closes it with the suite. It imports `testing`, and the binary never links it (`cmd/crewlet`'s `TestTheBinaryLinksNoTestScaffolding`) |
| `assistant/evals` | The assistant RUN WITHOUT A NODE, as PRODUCTION code: a real `assistant.Service` over in-memory stand-ins for its seams (`Store`, `Dispatcher`, `Revoker`, `Grant`, `Models`, `Budget`, `Audit`, `Metrics`, `Catalogue`), the ONE scripted provider (`Model`, `Round`), `Assemble` (the rig every suite and every case share, taking the App's real dispatcher and delegations for internal/api's suites), and the evaluation cases `crewlet assistant eval` reads (`LoadSuite`, strict through `config.ParseMember`) and judges (`RunSuite`). It never imports `testing` or `net/http/httptest`, so the binary links it and no test scaffolding; it answers a case's seeded routes with net/http's own pattern matching on a request value, never a transport (the dispatcher walk reads this package too). An evaluation measures what a model does with the tools it is offered, never the API's authority, which internal/api's suites hold |
| `api/assistantapi` | `/assistant/`: the routes a person's drawer uses, guarded, mounted on the authority table and refusing in the engine's envelope. An APPROVAL IS THE PERSON'S, FROM THEIR OWN BROWSER (ADR-0051): their cookie, a matching Origin, the digest the card showed, an arbitrated decision, a sixty-second single-use exec grant bound to the one request, and the proposal's own operation key, with a step-up answered on the approval itself. Every route answers 404 for what the caller does not own, and none is reachable through an assistant grant, so the assistant can never approve its own proposal. A person's CONVERSATIONS are its routes too: list, create, read (cards re-checked against the apps the reader holds now), clear, ask (deriving the turn's read grant from that very request), stop, the turn's server-sent stream (`Last-Event-ID` resumes it; a client falling 512 frames behind is disconnected) a table card's export from its stored snapshot, and a conversation's FILES: an image uploaded as the raw body, TYPED BY ITS BYTES against the attachable image types (HTML named `.png` is refused `415 assistant_file_refused`) and bounded at five mebibytes before it is read whole, and the owner's download of any file the conversation keeps, each on `assistant.converse` or `assistant.conversation.read`; an administrator's compliance download of one file is recorded and audited exactly as the transcript's compliance read is. EVERY DOWNLOAD IS AN ATTACHMENT of its sniffed type with `nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox` and `private, no-store`, so a kept file is never rendered as a page of this origin. `Viewer` is the `assistant` member of `/viewer`. A CONDITION OF THE NODE IS TOLD AS ONE (`operator.AnswerCondition`): `503 unavailable` in a person's words under its own hint, none where no wait clears it; anything no condition claims is `500 internal_error`, and a write whose outcome is unknown answers `outcome: unknown` with its key, while a read whose own write of the engine's nobody can confirm (a lapsed turn closed as its transcript is read) is `503 unavailable` under a wait, since asking again settles it. `assistant_unavailable` is left to the assistant's own reasons (no model chain it can build, a node shutting down, a runner it cannot reach) and carries no Retry-After |
| `api/secretsapi` | `/secrets`: the company's credentials, written through a running node because the coordination broker is inside its process and listens on no socket (a `crewlet` command run with the engine STOPPED reaches the same store itself, through `engine.OpenFleetStore`). EVERY ROUTE IS `admin`'s, through `authz.Router` and THREE VERBS the one permission admits: the listing and a row's metadata `secrets.list`, every write and the rekey `secrets.write` (a recent proof), and the TWO routes that return values, both decided on `secrets.reveal` BEFORE the store is read (a recent proof, and a PERSON present: a machine token is refused it on every arm) and each logging every name it reveals (`secret_revealed`, `secrets_revealed`): `GET /secrets/{name}`, which needs an explicit `?reveal=true` and asks the reveal's verb on top of the metadata's, and `POST /secrets/reveal`, the values of the names its body asks for (at most 64, `MaxRevealNames`, each in the `${VAR}` grammar, a name outside it refused by `secrets.CheckName` rather than answered missing, which is what bounds the answer), whose one reader is a CLI run resolving a company document through a node, handed exactly the names it asks for (the variables its command reads that the node lists, and the planned seats' tokens its `-secret-store` sink reads back, a batch at a time, so `GET ?reveal=true` is left to `crewlet secrets get -reveal`) and never the store, three verbs rather than one because what differs between them is the proof and the presence each row asks, and the table decides those rather than a check here that could drift from it. The routes used to decide nothing: the guard answers only whether somebody resolved, which meant "the operator" while a Tier A token was the only credential and meant "anybody" once a person could sign in holding a read-only permission alone. And THE ENGINE'S OWN KEYS ARE UNREACHABLE HERE: every route that takes a name answers a reserved one `403 reserved_name` before anything else, whatever the caller holds, and every refusal here is the engine's envelope, its codes in `httpjson`'s vocabulary with a sentence each, where the surface wrote bare `{"error": …}` maps no screen had words for, and the listing COUNTS them per keyring key (`engine_keys`) without naming any, the blind-index key was once an ordinary name here, which made a DELETE a way to orphan every address in the directory. And `POST /secrets/rekey` is the WHOLE rotation: the keyring that seals the bucket also seals every person's name, address and second factor in the identity estate, whose rows only a record can change, so it moves the store's rows at the version it read and the identity estate's through the node's own writer (the `Identity` seam), and the listing counts both (`identity_values`), because the answer an operator retires the old key on is "nothing is under it". The listing counts the Dashboard Assistant's sealed values per key too (`assistant_values`, the `Assistant` seam), which a rotation waits out rather than moves, because each leaves only with its conversation and a key dropped under a live conversation leaves it unreadable. A CONDITION OF THE NODE IS A `503` HERE TOO (`fail`, through `operator.AnswerCondition`): a coordination store that did not answer, or the identity estate refusing the read the listing counts sealed values by, in a person's words and under its own hint, never `500 internal_error`. And A WRITE RE-ACTIVATES THE REVISION THAT READS IT (ADR-0070): `PUT /secrets/{name}`, `DELETE /secrets/{name}` and the batch `PUT /secrets` (at most 64 values, refused whole on any bad name or value) ask the config surface which paths of the active revision read each name (the `Reactivator` seam, `configapi.Service.Reads` and `Reload`), ask `config.write` before sealing when any does, seal, and re-activate ONCE, synchronously, answering `read_by` and `reload` (`configapi.Reloaded`) with the status at 200 whatever the reload did, because the value is sealed first and crewlet-operator reads exactly 200; a value sealed and left there used to change nothing that was running |
| `api/stream` | The `/ws/stream` socket: pushes, plus a request/response query channel that is a thin adapter over the SAME function each REST route calls. WHO IS ASKING IS THE GUARD'S ANSWER, read from the context: the handler used to re-read the credential through the Tier A arm alone, so a browser signed in with a session cookie was refused here while every REST route beside it served them — and no identity travels beside the query as an argument, because two identities on one call are two chances to answer about different people. The in-flight query budget is PER SOCKET (`MaxInFlightQueries`, four): a tab's share of the reader floor `internal/store` derives as "two full dashboards at four queries each", so the two are one decision stated twice, and a person's tabs each have their own. A burst past the pool waits in the pool's own queue, and the one read that must never wait behind it — resolving who is acting — runs on the store's reserved connection; that reserve is why the per-PRINCIPAL budget it replaced, refcounted across a person's sockets behind a `sync.OnceFunc` release, was machinery that bought nothing. The HANDSHAKE'S `Origin` is judged by the writes' own rule (`auth.CSRF.RefuseCrossSite`), because a WebSocket handshake is a GET the write gate passes as a read while the socket it opens streams the company's state, and the library's own check compared `Origin` with the request's `Host` — refusing a second hostname in `api.auth.allowed_origins` and every browser behind a proxy that rewrites `Host`. A socket is AUTHENTICATED AT ITS HANDSHAKE AND ENDED BY WHAT ENDS ITS CREDENTIAL (`lifetime.go`), because a handshake decision alone let a revoked session's tab go on receiving the company's state for as long as it stayed open — and never on a timer of its own: the 60-second re-check it replaced read the directory per tab per minute and still let a revocation stand for that minute. Each event is handled the cheapest correct way. An identity move that NAMES the credential — a session's lineage, the person's id, or the login a Tier A token's binding row holds, `token:<id>` (`CredentialsMoved`, every node serving the API hearing its own applier) — decides exactly the sockets it names by the guard (`auth.Guard.ResolveOpen`, a session's idle deadline set aside — a socket can never be re-issued a cookie, and an open live view is activity), each on its own goroutine with a direct read. A move that NAMES NOBODY — the fleet session generation, a record the node retained, a replaced estate, the node passing the stall grace (`identityvouch.go`) — closes EVERY socket 1013 (try again later) WITHOUT READING ANYTHING, and the reconnect's handshake decides: a read per socket there was one identity read per open tab queued at once on the store's one reserved identity connection, and the per-credential decision queue built to soften it (`decisions.go`) is gone with it. A published company is decided IN MEMORY against the roster just published (`Options.SeatOf`): a seat that company no longer holds as a human seat closes 4403, a seat that answers to another handle 1013, and the watch is decided again, with no identity read. The credential's own end is a timer per socket (`auth.Lifetime`), re-armed while the guard still serves it, and every socket is decided once as it starts listening, so a record that landed between the handshake and that moment is not missed. A credential that names nobody closes 4401, a person whose seat is gone 4403, and one this node cannot decide 1013, so the reconnect's handshake answers 503 before any snapshot — there is no `identity` frame and no degraded hold. A decision that resolves SOMEBODY ELSE — another login, another seat or none, other grants — closes 1013 too, so the reconnect's handshake builds the snapshot for the new grants and the tab asks who it is (`viewer`) again: kept open, the socket answered the new principal while the tab went on naming the old one, and a snapshot re-sent in place for a changed audience moved the pushes and left that answer behind. WHAT a socket receives is decided per KIND: every push kind declares the NEED its question takes beside its route (`stream.Need`: `event` is an `events` row, so `admin`'s; `inbox_changed` answers `work_inbox`, so Worklet's; every other company kind is the OVERVIEW, everybody's — ADR-0033), a client carries the AUDIENCE its principal's permissions describe, and what of the overview it receives is decided PER PAYLOAD (`stream.Shaped`) — a seat's live call masked to its status for a reader that seat's trail is not open to, by the rule `audit.seat.read` decides, and the snapshot — and its four REST mirrors, `/agents`, `/org`, `/tools` and `/stream/snapshot` — is built for that audience. It used to decide only that the caller was somebody, which was harmless while every credential was an operator token and a firehose the day a session cookie resolved: a person who could only write work items received every phase's prompt and response. Targeted kinds are ROOM-ROUTED through ONE keyed index (`RoomKey`, `seat:<handle>` or `artifact:<id>`), and a room frame with no room is dropped, never broadcast. `inbox_changed` is the seat room's kind, pushed by `InboxChanged` (timed into change visibility from the movement's broker instant, which the frame does not carry) to the sockets whose `watch` of that seat was ALLOWED — the watch's name RESOLVED first through `iam.OwnerOf` and the same directory `work_inbox` reads, so a login is installed on the record its holder's notices are pushed to rather than on the login as sent (where no frame ever arrives, and on which a lead was refused as leading nobody), and the directory asked only once the watch is decided as far as it can be without it; then decided with `authz.ActionPersonRead`, the rule `work_inbox` takes about the same seat (its holder or whoever leads it, holding Worklet, or `admin`), and three-valued: a refusal is an error frame on a socket that stays open, never a 4403, which the dashboard reads as the whole channel withdrawn; an undecidable answer installs nothing; and every decision of the socket's credential, and every published company, re-decides the watch, withdrawing it only on a refusal. AN ARTIFACT'S ROOM (`rooms.go`) is joined by `artifact_join`, decided like reading the artifact (`get_artifact`, three-valued, through `Options.Rooms.Access`; refused is `not_found` — `artifact_trashed` for an artifact disclosed to the reader and in the trash, the code a datastore question about it answers too, as the REST routes' `409` — undecidable `unavailable` with its hint, and neither closes the socket) and answered with its roster in the presence hub's delivery order (the socket joined and answered inside `presence.Hub.Join`'s hand, as a chat room's join is, so no `artifact_presence` frame overtakes the answer and none taken before it is lost), left by `artifact_leave`, told what the socket is doing by `presence` frames (held to ten a second per socket on the read loop, then handed with the joins to ONE room goroutine per socket so a leave never overtakes its join), and pushed `artifact_changed`, `artifact_data` and `artifact_presence`, the first two from the appliers the engine's live relay says this node hears (its own while its copy serves, a sound peer's while it does not) and the third from its presence hub. A join is DECIDED AGAIN on every decision of the credential and on every share, trash, transfer, purge or reset of the artifact: the movement SUSPENDS the room's datastore pushes on the applier's goroutine and a goroutine of the service's decides each member, withdrawing a refused one with an error frame naming the artifact, keeping an undecidable one, and telling a kept one that missed a push to re-read. A datastore push is SHAPED PER READER (`artifactdata.Readable` over the role each join decided and the reader's record, one encode per set of rows a reader may see, a reader who may see none sent nothing) and names its documents instead of carrying them past `PushFrameMaxBytes`. Every switch of the relay's source reaches the rooms as the two domains' own reset, which pushes every room a `reset`, decides it again and tells its datastore to re-read. At most `MaxRoomsPerSocket` rooms per socket. Two walks hold the kinds: every room-routed kind needs a `PushSeat` or `PushArtifact` producer, and the dashboard's `PushKind` union equals the declared kinds in both directions. A refusal on AUTHORITY — a query's or a watch's — carries the rule's `reason` and the `grants` that would have admitted the caller (and an artifact's needed `role`) on its `unauthorized` error frame, under the REST envelope's own keys, worded by `authz.RefusalFields` through `RefusedFor` and never spelled here — no relation to an artifact is `not_found` with nothing beside it, and a code the mapping gives that this vocabulary lacks (`step_up_required`, `token_refused`) is the fault it would be: the app used to reduce it to the question's name on the way here, so the dashboard's live data channel was the one transport that could not say what would change the answer. CHAT'S LIVE HALF IS THE `ChatHub` (`chatpush.go`), and EVERY FRAME IS FILTERED BY ITS RECIPIENT'S OWN VISIBILITY before it leaves: the eight `chat_*` kinds need the reader's `chatlet` (`NeedChatlet`; an audience's app terms are DERIVED from `iam.Apps`, so the next app is one line in `iam` rather than five edits here), are routed `RouteViewer`, which `Hub.Broadcast` refuses, and are held to the reader's need and posture FIRST (`Client.admits`, so a socket a frame will never reach costs no visibility read), then decided per recipient by `chatlet.Visible` at the committed batch's own position, then leave by the ONE fan-out every push takes (`fanOut`: need, posture, shape, one encode per posture), so a degraded node sends none. The viewer is the seat `iam.ActorFor` names the socket's principal as — the function a chat write's author comes from — decided ONCE at the join and never re-resolved, because a principal that moves closes the socket (`lifetime.go`). A person's read state is pushed to their own sockets from a WATCH of the coordination record on every node serving sockets, never from a scatter the writer sends. A CHAT ROOM'S PRESENCE (who is viewing, typing, working) is the presence hub's (`internal/presence`, ADR-0038) like an artifact's: the chat room's `presence` frame (`room: "chat"`, `channel_id`) IS the join — a tab looks at one chat room at a time, so a frame naming another room moves the socket and an empty one leaves — admitted once `chatlet.Visible` lets its viewer see the room (the decision a committed frame takes, so a refusal leaves nothing and says nothing), pushed as `chat_presence` from the hub's fan-out to the sockets in the room and nobody else — its roster handed in the presence hub's delivery order (`presence.Hub.Join`'s hand, which places the socket and sends the roster under the chat hub's ONE LOCK, the lock every fan-out of the room's presence chooses its sockets and sends under), so a socket is shown a fan-out wholly before its roster or after it and nothing after the roster that took it out — and decided again by every committed record that narrows the room, which takes out whoever it shuts out with a roster naming nobody, and by every gap, which may have held such a record and so decides every socket in a room again — keeping one it cannot decide, where the record fails closed, because a gap is only the possibility of a change; a seat's working indicator is its agent member there (`SetStatus` touches, `ClearStatus` withdraws), raised on whichever node runs the turn and carried between nodes sealed. The unsigned fleet-wide scatter it replaced, `crewlet.chat.presence`, let anybody on the cluster port show anybody typing in any room. A batch the hub cannot keep up with is dropped and owes every screen a `chat_room_changed` naming no room, which is also how a gap in what the engine hears is told. The hub is the PROCESS's (`api.EngineChatLive`), attached to the engine after `New` on every node, and only an API mounted over it makes it say it serves sockets |
| `observe` | The observability edge, and the two routes are deliberately different: the STORE is written by a publish listener inline on the publishing node (no consumer group, so no two nodes can write one row); the PROJECTION is fed by an ephemeral per-caller broadcast. A node WITHOUT `data` writes no rows of its own — its store is deleted at every boot — so `Custody` publishes its events in batches onto a durable custody topic instead, buffered so a publish never waits and loud about what a full buffer drops, and every data node's `Keeper` takes them from ONE fleet-wide group. A group delivers a batch whose acknowledgement was lost again, to anybody, so a keeper writes a batch FIRST and claims it create-only in coordination SECOND: the winner keeps it, any other writer deletes its copy, and a crash between the two is settled from the node's `custody_unsettled` rows at its next pass — because a batch in two logs is a stateless node's spend the usage domain bills twice, and claimed first, a claimant that died before writing would hold a batch nobody wrote. A REDELIVERY OF A BATCH THIS NODE ALREADY SETTLED WRITES NOTHING (`custody_settled`, swept with the event log): written again it was in flight again, and its kept rows left every usage count until the claim answered a second time. And a keeper's stop LEAVES the group — stops taking batches, gives one in hand a second to settle, detaches — before the usage publisher's last flush, since every settle as kept makes rows count on this node's days |
| `eventfan` | The fleet's TURN-LEVEL HISTORY, read from every data node at query time (ADR-0021). An event is written once, inline, to the store of the data node that published it or keeps it in custody for a node without `data`, so no one store is the company's history: `events`, `event`, `event_series`, `trace`, `turn`, `turns`, `phases`, `notification_outcomes`, a seat's history and the live projection's boot seed (its feed, its spend window through `phase_tokens`, each seat's last turn) are asked of every live data node over the queue's EPHEMERAL verbs on `crewlet.observe.read` — the asker reads its own store directly and in parallel — and merged, with ONE `coverage{nodes:[{id,answered,error}],complete}` on every answer naming a node that did not answer inside the two-second `FleetReadBudget` (which the `history_partial` alarm borrows). The merges are PURE and each is exact for disjoint stores for a reason it states: a keyset page stops at the newest point any FULL part stopped at (a reply cut to fit the transport is full and short), a histogram's window is pinned to the asker's clock before bars are summed, and `turns` is TWO scatters — every data node's page, then every data node's share of exactly the turns listed — because the node a turn is selected on is not always the only node holding it. The interface `internal/api/queries` reads it through returns a coverage from every method, so a bare `*store.EventLog` does not compile there. A seat's history is asked by its AGENT ID and never by a role name, and every question the reply is drawn against carries the ONE instant it is answered at (`q.At`), so a node never labels its part by its own clock. A departed node's detail is gone, by design; the aggregates survive it in `usage`. `eventfantest` certifies it on the twin and on a three-member cluster |
| `tokens` | Rolls LLM spend into the breakdown a dashboard renders — by phase, model, provider entry, worker and seat, plus a per-turn tail on the LIVE window only — and its time axis. ONE IMPLEMENTATION is the whole point: this aggregation had three copies once, and a refresh routinely disagreed with the page it replaced. TWO INPUTS, one shape: the live projection's spend records (`Aggregate`) and every NAMED window's company days from the replicated `usage` domain (`FoldDaily`, `BucketDaily`), which is why a named window has no turn and no hour — a day's row holds neither, and inventing them from this node's event log is the one-node answer ADR-0020 replaced. A phase chart draws FOUR bands folded once in `PhaseBand` — a launch of an executor's run is no fifth: both producers file its record under the executor's own phase (`PhaseExecute`) — a window is at most `MaxSpendRangeDays` (90) and one reaching past the 181-day horizon is REFUSED rather than drawn short. `calls` is PROVIDER CALLS on every bucket (`PhaseCalls` is the one rule both producers count a phase by: a phase's rounds, and NONE for the executor's own record — every executor is its harness's run, so the engine made no call for it, and counting the record's one round put two calls on every turn that nobody made; `LaunchCalls` is a launch's: its harness's own turns as reported on its `executor_collected` record), and a TURN'S COST is defined here once (`Record.InTurn`): its phases, its executor's launches and its in-turn auxiliary spend, never the reflection after it. A leaf in everything but the calendar (`period`), because both the live projection and the query surface need it. A seat's band is keyed on its AGENT ID and a unit's on its KEY, each carrying a `label` drawn from the company as the caller holds it now, because a name is prose two seats may share — keyed on it, two namesakes pooled their spend into one row — and a PERSON's auxiliary spend is a row of its own, keyed on the HUMAN SEAT'S HANDLE, since it was charged to no agent |
| `auxspend` | What the seats' AUXILIARY model spends, recorded — the `Use` every caller of the auxiliary seam states (stage, purpose, turn; the zero value REFUSED), the per-turn `Tally` a turn's work item is charged from (ADR-0022), the turn's own `Budget` meter a turn-stage call is charged THROUGH and asks before it is made — because a post-charge beside the meter left the window it filled unseen, and the turn's next round was sent, billed and refused — and the node's `Ledger`, which coalesces each call into one `auxiliary_spend` record per (stage, seat or person, turn, purpose, model, provider entry, company day) per flush. RECORDED INDEPENDENTLY OF THE COUNTER at the one seam that charges it, because the counter exists only where a coordination store and a budget scope do, and a figure a person reads must depend on neither. Flushed every 15 s (the usage domain's cadence, held equal by its suite), for a turn before its end is published and before its reflection pass's sentinel, and at the stop — ordered after every producer of auxiliary calls and before the custody flush and the stream close. A refused publish is kept SEALED, its id and instant fixed so a retry is the same event, and the first refusal ends a flush, up to `MaxPending` — so a process that dies during a broker outage loses that backlog as well as its open buckets |
| `tracing` | The OpenTelemetry wiring: ONE TracerProvider, built from the standard `OTEL_*` environment (never a Tier A block), and the conversion between OTel's `context.Context` carrier and the `trace_id`/`span_id`/`parent_span_id` the event envelope has always had. The provider is installed UNCONDITIONALLY and only the exporter is optional, so ids reach the event store whether or not a collector exists — and its own `init` installs a working one, because OTel's built-in default is a no-op that passes the parent's span context through and would silently reinstate the bug this package removed |
| `notify` | The backend-neutral notification spine — conversation keys, digest coalescing, party resolution, the rate valve, and BOTH DIRECTIONS across a chat surface: the working indicator writes, and `ThreadReader`/`ThreadReaders` read a thread back for the turn-start block. Each direction is a per-backend seam with a fan-out that routes on the trigger's own `transport`, because a company runs two chat surfaces and a caller holding one backend's reader silently reads none. An indicator is posted as a `StatusPost` (handle, channel, thread, the text, and the NEWEST turn holding it, which a backend that can name a turn links to), and Chatlet shows it as the seat's agent member of the room's presence rather than a message. `ThreadOf` is deliberately NOT `ConversationOf`: one answers where a reply lands (a top-level message anchors on its own id), the other what was already said (a message with no thread has no answer). Built before any vendor sat on it, because a spine built after its first vendor has that vendor welded into it. AN ENGINE-HOSTED SOURCE'S SENDER ID IS THE SEAT'S HANDLE (the tracker's, the knowledge base's, chat's), and the registry is built with those sources DECLARED as namespaces whose ids are handles (`NewRegistry`'s `handleNamespaces`, the engine's one `firstPartySources` list, held to its native parsers by a test), so `ByExternalID` on one answers `ByHandle` and nothing can be registered there: read through a registry that did not know, every person on a native surface was an outside party — the learning profile filed them under a platform id, the thread block fenced a colleague as `external`, and a turn a person's comment woke was a stranger's, refused every artifact read the requester ceiling would have allowed them. EVERY WAKE'S BODY IS FENCED BY THE SPINE, never by a prompt (ADR-0040): `Render`, the one caller of `Prompt.Build`, hands a prompt the body already inside a fence, so a prompt that renders it in six openers cannot render it bare in any. What the body is and who wrote it is the ninth question a source answers (`Prompt.Provenance`), and most answer from what their parser STATED (`Stated`, over `BodySourceField`, `BodyAuthorField` and a first-party record's `BodyAuthorKindField`): an author only where the payload says one person wrote every word — a comment or a review just written, a message, an item's text on the event that created it — and otherwise nobody, which reads `external` on a vendor's surface, because the person who caused an event (who assigned the issue) is not who wrote its body; the engine's own tracker, knowledge base and chat read it as a colleague's (`StatedHosted`, `ChatPrompt.Hosted`, `Transcript.Hosted`: `Provenance.Hosted`), the one rule for a surface the engine hosts (`provenance.TrustForHosted`), as the same text reads through a search hit or a read tool (`engine.TestEveryWakeSurfaceReadsAnUnnamedWriterByWhoHostsIt`), and a first-party record naming its writer by kind alone (the engine, an operator credential) names that kind. A first-party writer the chart no longer holds is the kind its record stated (`FirstPartyAuthor`). The delivery mints a token for that wake alone (`Inbound.Fence`, recorded as `types.ExternalNotification.FenceBoundary`), because no turn exists yet to lend one, a coalesced burst's digest moves every earlier body's fence onto the latest's token, and the turn moves them onto its own (`provenance.Rekey`, in `engine.DescribeTrigger`), escaping everything else a trigger carries as a tool answer's other strings are; `engine.TestEveryWakeSurfaceFencesWhatSomebodyWrote` holds every surface the engine hosts or integrates to it, and `engine.TestEveryPromptBuilderIsAWakeSurface` walks the tree for every type that builds a trigger (a `Build(notify.Inbound, …)` method) and holds each to one of those surfaces. `SalientLabels` is that same answer for a DELIVERED notification's salient body, one `provenance.Label` per message in the order the turn reads them, and `SalientLabel` their merge (`provenance.Merge`), which the turn-start judgements read the turn's ask under: a merged burst names every constituent's writers at the least trust any earns (`engine.TestTheTurnsAskIsFencedAsItsTriggerIs`). `Prompt.WakesActor` is asked with the whole notification, since Worklet answers it from the routing reason on each recipient's copy rather than the event type every copy shares. A human seat's contact identities are registered only while the person the IDENTITY DIRECTORY binds to it may be reached: a `Standing` is ONE reading of the directory, applied whole to the registry being built, and its zero value is CHART-ONLY, which no running node builds from: a node that HOLDS THE ESTATE reads its OWN identity rows, from boot, company or none, and a node WITHOUT `data` reads the same reading through the estate router (`SeatHolders`, floored on the identity log, so a data node behind answers unknown rather than an old binding) — because routing by the chart attributes a suspended person's word to their seat for every delivery a node wins, and a node that has NEVER read a standing withholds every human seat until it does. The zero value is what an engine with no directory at all builds from, which is `crewlet validate` and a test. A binding names its seat by its handle (ADR-0013), so a reading finds the seat exactly as the request path finds the same binding's seat. What AGENTS are shown follows the same reading: a turn pins the live registry's withholding beside its org (`turnctx.Turn.Withheld`), so a lead's roster and `lookup_colleague` leave out exactly the people an inbound message would not be attributed to, and neither says why. `active`, `invited` and `enrolling` route; `suspended`, `retired`, a removal and a stage this build cannot name withhold, and an unreadable directory is an error rather than a reading, so a caller never hands every suspended person's seat back to the chart over an outage. The CHAT PROMPT reads the same addressed rule the indicator and the delivery check do (`addressOf`), and on an addressed message states that verdict IN PLACE OF the triage: the triage tells an unaddressed seat that silence beats noise, which on an addressed message is the one answer the decoder refuses |
| `backup` | A restorable copy of everything a node holds, taken from INSIDE the engine because that is the only place both estates are reachable: the store file is locked to this process and the driver refuses a second opener, and the embedded broker binds no socket for any outside tool to dial. The store copy is `VACUUM INTO` (the backup API is stubbed and plain `VACUUM` is behind an experimental flag) and is VERIFIED before it counts; the streams — mailboxes and every coordination bucket alike, since a bucket IS a stream — go over the JetStream wire API, because the vendored client has no snapshot call at all. A stream is copied WITH its consumers, and the broker abandons a snapshot when one of them ends mid-copy, which every coordination listing does (an ephemeral watch consumer); so a stream is snapshotted again when the consumer the broker names is one it no longer holds, five attempts in all (`backup_stream_retaken`), and every other abandonment fails the backup. The MANIFEST IS THE CLAIM: written last, and a directory without one is the debris of a run that did not finish rather than a partial backup |
| `maintenance` | The retention sweep for the tables that answer "recently" rather than "ever" — fire claims, channel membership, counterparty profiles, the conversation ledger, each state-log domain's OPERATION LEDGER, the coordination store's REMOVAL MARKERS (a bucket no clock ages keeps every delete marker, and every listing that meets one re-reads it from the leader), and a removed human seat's CHAT READ STATE (its bucket has no age either, so a record whose seat the company the fleet is pointed at has lacked for a day — stamped when a sweep first observes the absence, the mailbox retirement's grace so a seat restored inside it gets back its mail and its rooms together — is forgotten by a compare-and-set that checks the stamp; the roster is the HUMAN seats, because the record is the seat's and a person leaving a seat the company still has leaves it to the next holder; a roster that cannot be read forgets nobody). Every one of their migrations says they are swept and ships the index a range delete needs — a table with a documented retention and no sweep grows for the life of the deployment, which is what the `<domain>_ops` tables did until `StatelogJobs` existed. A ledger horizon is the DOMAIN's, and a domain may declare a SHORTER one per subject KIND whose writers never re-ask late (`OpsHorizon.Kinds`, a job of its own) — the identity estate's sessions go in an hour where everything else keeps the month, a range over the stored subject on an index only that domain ships. Every horizon is a FUNCTION read at each sweep (`Horizon`, `Fixed` for a constant), so a retention a revision changes is the next sweep's rather than the next boot's. A fleet singleton, because N nodes scanning the same rows is waste rather than corruption — except where the rows are a table each node owns its own copy of, which is what a job's `Scope` of `NodeLocal` is for: swept under the singleton, such a table is tidied on one node and grows for ever on every other, and that looks exactly like a sweep that works to whoever checks the node it ran on |
| `mattermost`, `slack`, `gitlab`, `github`, `jira`, `confluence`, `datadog`, `atlassian` | The vendors. Each contributes only what is genuinely its own: a client, a parser, a transport, a prompt, a provisioning reconcile — and no more, which is why Jira has no transport (an agent writes through its own MCP tools) and why its reconcile and GitHub's report rather than mint (neither vendor issues a credential on a provisioner's behalf). Datadog is the ALERT one and the shape shows it: a monitor is addressed to nobody, so it routes on the monitor's own TAGS with a company-wide fallback seat, and its deliveries cannot be signed at all. Slack is the one with no reconciler — its apps are created from the command line — so it is registered teardown-only and carries no reconcile status. A vendor's PROMPT states only what the engine knows and the model cannot — why the seat is reading this, the item by the reference a person would paste, the vendor facts (mention markup, subscription mechanics, what a merge or a close means) and how a seat declines what it HOLDS on that surface (reassign the item assigned to it, hand a review request on) — never offered to a seat asked about somebody else's item, which is not its to move — while "stay silent" is shown only to a seat the prompt's own `Addressed` reads as NOT asked. How to review a diff or work an alert is the model's own, and that an ask may not end in silence is the executor's contract and the decoder's refusal, so neither is restated per vendor |
| `integration` | The app-neutral reconcile spine, and the reason there are eight vendor packages rather than eight loops. It owns the vocabulary a pass reports in — `Finding`, its severity ordering, and the `Phase`/`Actor` a `Report` classifies to — the schedule that follows WHO HAS TO ACT rather than what failed, the fleet-singleton `Worker` that runs every registered surface, and the disconnect intent a teardown is driven by — WHO ASKED included (`State.RequestedBy`), because the loop carries the teardown out minutes later on whichever node holds its duty, and the revision that drops the block was recorded as the loop's own while a forced disconnect recorded the person. It also decides the TOOL ROLL-UP (`Rollup`) — one state per tool the `integrations` answer carries — because a screen that re-derived it kept a second copy of the phase order and drifted. `integrationtest` is its certification suite: one set of cases every reconciler passes, in the `queuetest`/`coordtest` tradition, because a promise about what a pass does to somebody's Slack workspace written down and checked by nobody is the worst shape a rule can be in A FORCED LOOK AT A HELD SURFACE STAYS OWED (`Worker.visit`): an apply's sweep or a refresh that finds another writer holding the lease records nothing and re-arms the look for the next tick, since the ask was spent on the way there and dropping it left the surface on its settled cadence |
| `fleetsecrets` | The company's credential store: this package owns the KEY, coordination owns the BYTES. THE ONLY STORE: there is no node-local half for the engine to carry here at boot — a `crewlet` command run with the engine stopped writes this store too, through the node's own broker (`engine.OpenFleetStore`), because a command that read a table of the node's own with the engine stopped read every value the fleet held as unset, and a provisioner mints over unset. `Store.Values` answers the names asked and only those, in one round trip, for the harness's batched read, through a node's reveal or directly with the engine stopped. TWO VIEWS OVER ONE BUCKET: `Store` is the OPERATOR's, and it neither lists, snapshots, reads, writes nor deletes a name in the engine's own namespace (`secrets.Reserved` — `iam/…`, the identity directory's blind-index key), answering `secrets.ErrReservedName`; `Estate` is the ENGINE's, and it addresses those names and nothing else. They shared the environment-variable grammar once, which made the engine's key listable, revealable, deletable and decrypted into each node's `${VAR}` snapshot on every apply. A REKEY is the one gesture that crosses — the keyring is one keyring — and it COUNTS the engine's rows rather than naming them, and leaves every row's provenance as it found it: it re-seals a value it did not choose, and stamping its own caller over each row made every credential in the company read as set by whoever rotated the keyring. The engine's view writes its key CONDITIONALLY, `Estate.Create` only where none is, so two minters keep one key; and a REKEY re-seals every row at the version it read, because a plain put of what it read reverted an operator's rotation that landed in between |
| `compact` | Making text fit a budget by having a model REWRITE it, never by cutting it. A cut keeps whatever came first and drops the rest unread, which for text a model acts on is a different message: a comment cut at its cap reads as a comment that ended there, a runbook cut at four kilobytes as a runbook with no step four, an error page cut at two hundred runes as its HTTP preamble without the line naming the missing scope. So where a bound is genuine — a block re-sent every round, a document bigger than the model answering from it can hold — the over-budget text goes to the seat's AUXILIARY chain (`llm_auxiliary`, falling back to `llm`) through the same metered seam the learning workers use, and comes back rewritten to fit with identifiers verbatim. WHETHER a bound is needed is the caller's question, and most of what used to be cut needed none: something upstream already bounded it, or a reader could page through it whole. WHAT TO DO WHEN NO REWRITE CAN BE HAD is the caller's too — drop whole entries and say how many, name a payload by size and digest (`Omitted`), carry a draft whole — and the one fallback no caller may take is the cut this replaced, which is why `Fit` returns an error rather than a degraded string. Text that already fits costs no call; a rewrite is cached by its input in the engine's one `Cache`, so a block re-rendered every round pays once; an input past `MaxChunks` × `ChunkBytes` is refused (`ErrTooLarge`) rather than summarised at a cost nobody priced. A REWRITE READS ITS TEXT AS SOMEBODY'S (ADR-0040): every completion fences it under a token minted for that call, labelled as the caller says (`Request.Label`, `Bound.Labelled` — a thread's speakers, a page's writers, a past ask's label; the zero label reads as nothing vouched for), ends its system prompt with `provenance.StandingInstruction` and neutralises its answer under that token, because what it is handed is often a stranger's and what it writes lands where that text was going; it was bracketed by a FIXED `<<<TEXT`/`TEXT>>>` marker, the very delimiter the record rejects, which a text spelling the closer stepped straight out of |
| `textcut` | Shortening a string without breaking it. It replaced FOUR helpers — three named `truncate`, in `agent/extension`, `learning` and `providers/llm/harness`, plus `learning`'s own `preview` — which were four copies of one rule already disagreeing on the easy half: two appended `…` and two appended `...`. A fifth, the sandbox runner's private rune-counted tail, became `Tail` — the one cut that keeps the END, for a process's account of itself. The hard half is that a plain `s[:n]` is invalid UTF-8 whenever a multi-byte rune straddles the cut, which a JSON encoder substitutes, a model reads as a replacement character and a vendor rejects. BYTES ONLY, because cutting is the last resort rather than the first: content a turn reasons over is passed whole, and a value with a vendor limit is REFUSED naming the field rather than silently cut to fit. Text a model or a person acts on is never cut here: where a bound is genuine it goes to `compact` and is rewritten to fit, is refused naming the limit, or is described by its size. What is left is a diagnostic or a process's tail past a transport ceiling, a MARKED preview of something the reader can open (a search snippet, an inbox excerpt), an identifier with a length limit, which keeps a digest of the whole beside the cut, and EMBEDDING INPUT, which no reader sees — bounded by the model and cut, unmarked, by `providers/embeddings`' rule |
| `tabular` | A table of rows as a DOWNLOAD, CSV and XLSX, one implementation of each (ADR-0008): a cell a spreadsheet would run as a formula (opening with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return, and not a plain number) is prefixed with an apostrophe in both, the rule moved out of `artifactdata`'s export so the datastore's export and the assistant's table card downloads share it; XLSX is minimal SpreadsheetML over `archive/zip` with the standard library alone; a table card downloads with its columns, rows, total and an `As of` row (`CSV`, `XLSX`, `Tables`), a cell past the format refused rather than cut, and a walk holds every CSV writer in the tree to this package |
| `words` | A SET IN A SENTENCE: `Or` says the alternatives a value comes from (`"html", "slides" or "doc"`), `And` a list read whole (`"k1" and "k2"`), and `AndMore` one too long to read whole (`"a", "b" and 3 more`), each member quoted as `%q` quotes the value beside it and an empty set said as `none`; a member that is not a value — a command in backticks, a `${VAR}` reference, a phrase, a field path written as the file writes it — is a `Said` and is joined the same way, written as it is. It exists because every fmt verb prints a slice as Go spells one, `[html slides doc]` under `%v` and `%s` and `["html" "slides"]` under `%q`, and that shape reached people and models at the end of a refusal on every domain that names a closed set — an artifact's kind, a datastore column's type, a page's status, a Tier A grant — and on CLI lines naming nodes; config's warnings and the query surface each had a private helper for it, both replaced. ITS SUITE IS A GATE over the whole module: every call shaped like `fmt.Sprintf` (`format string, args ...any`: `fmt.Errorf`, a package's `invalid()` and `fail()`, a problem list's `add`) is read WITH ITS TYPES, from export data `go list -export` answers with this test binary's toolchain, `-race` and `-tags`, and an operand a verb prints is refused when it is a slice, an array or a map — because the shape is the operand's type and never its spelling, and `artifacts.Kinds` reads like any identifier. So is every operand of a call that takes no format and prints as `%v` does — fmt's `Sprint`, `Print`, `Fprint` and `Append` families and the `log` package's `Print`, `Fatal` and `Panic` ones, named by the callee itself, since a function taking only `...any` is as often a logger's key-value pairs. Not judged: test files and test-support packages (a name ending in `test`), a type with its own `String`, `Error` or `Format`, a byte slice or array under `%s`, `%q`, `%x` or `%X`, `%T` and `%p`, an interface operand, a format that is not a constant and operands forwarded whole; and a walk that judged under 150 packages or 8,000 calls fails, since one reading nothing passes. The same walk refuses a LIST JOINED BY HAND into a sentence, in the four shapes every private copy of `Or` took: a `strings.Join` whose separator is a lower-case `or` or `and`, one written beside one (`strings.Join(set[:n-1], ", ") + " and " + last`), one joining all but the last member by commas (a conjunction held in a variable is still one), and a closed set joined by commas right after `one of`, `(want` or `offers`, which said it unquoted and with no `or`, whether the `strings.Join` sits beside the sentence or inside a function of the module that returns it, as config's `names()` did — and a walk that read under 300 joins fails. A list joined by commas alone and offered as no choice is not judged, since nothing in its shape says a reader reads it as a sentence rather than a column of names. And the suite holds every string literal in the trees a person reads the sentences of (`personFacing`: the API's details and hints, the CLI's lines, the configuration's problems, the tracker's tag refusals, and the files the retention gestures are decided in, whose refusals the API and the CLI answer word for word) to carry no em dash, outside a log line, a panic, a struct tag and an em dash standing alone as an empty table cell, the trees growing as the rest of the engine's person-facing sentences are rewritten; the dashboard's own gate is `app/copy.test.ts`, over everything it ships. A LEAF over the standard library, held to it by a test, admitted to the `iam` and `artifacts` leaf sets |
| `jsoncarry` | How a record ROUND-TRIPS through a build that only half understands it: a decode keeps the fields the struct has no home for, and the next encode folds them back in. ONE CLAUSE is the whole package and it is the one a second implementation gets backwards — A CARRIED FIELD LOSES TO A KNOWN ONE, because with the precedence the other way round a stale carried copy silently reverts the write that set the field, on exactly the path an upgrade is in progress on. It was written TWICE, in `doclet` and the org chart's domain (since removed), and the copies had already drifted (one decoder carried a fourth parameter the other did not have and neither read), which is `textcut`'s, `whsec`'s and `jsprovision`'s story arriving for a fourth time; a fifth state-log domain would have made three copies of a precedence rule nothing compares. THE KNOWN NAMES ARE READ OFF THE STRUCT'S TAGS (`Names`), by encoding/json's own rules, and never off a marshalled zero value: a zero value does not marshal an omitempty field, so every domain had to list those names by hand, four had been missed (a field on a person, on an invitation, on a unit and on a seat), and each missed field was decoded AND carried, so clearing it to its zero value re-encoded the stale carried copy straight back. A LEAF over `encoding/json` and `reflect` |
| `httprelay` | One HTTP exchange carried from the node that received it to the PROCESS that holds what answers it, over the queue's ephemeral request and reply — the tool bridge's, whose session is live state in the seat's holder while the request lands wherever a balancer or a stale address put it. OVER THE BROKER, NEVER A PROXY TO THE HOLDER'S ADDRESS: nodes have no HTTP address for each other, a node without ingress serves none, and a satellite accepts no inbound connection, while every node reaches the broker. The entry serves a SUBJECT PER EXCHANGE and sends OPEN to the holder's node subject (`crewlet.<name>.node.<node>`); the holder decides (`Admit`: serve, elsewhere, refuse), runs the request through its own handler, and PUSHES the response back as frames — status and header once, then the body as written — each acknowledged once the entry has written it to its client: STOP AND WAIT, which is the back-pressure that keeps a large answer whole in neither process, and a frame pushed again is written once by its sequence number. A subject per exchange because one registration answers at most `queue.MaxConcurrentAnswers` at once and an event stream lives for hours. An OPEN repeated after a lost reply names the same exchange and runs nothing twice. HEARTBEATS both ways (an empty frame every ten seconds, its acknowledgement back), three missed and the other side is gone: the holder cancels the request, the entry cuts the response (`http.ErrAbortHandler`) so a client never reads a cut stream as complete. Every message SIGNED (`signedask`, ADR-0031), because both directions are instructions on a broker that authenticates nobody, on the two channels a relay's name scopes (`<name>.open`, `<name>.frame`), which `New` and `Domains` build in one place so the engine's channel gate holds the domains a relay signs under |
| `plainjson` | JSON READ AS TEXT, one encoder: `Marshal(v, indent)` writes `<`, `>` and `&` as themselves and ends where its value does, and is otherwise byte for byte `json.Marshal`'s (or `json.MarshalIndent`'s under an indent). `json.Marshal` escapes those three into `\u003c` and its kin so its output can sit inside an HTML document, and nothing here is one: a tool call's RECORD (`tools.RecordArgs`) is read by a person in a `pre`, where a URL argument read `?a=1\u0026b=2`; a TOOL ANSWER (the builtins, a delegated worker's report) and a PRIOR-WORK LINE (`agent/ledger`) are read by a model, which was shown `if a < b` as six-byte escapes it had to decode back and charged five bytes of context for each; and a harness run's transcript (`providers/llm/harness`) shows the model its own earlier calls, which it reads as written, so it was shown its own `<` as an escape; and a command's `-json` is read at a terminal, by a script and by the loop fixing a document from `crewlet validate -json`, so every one goes through ONE printer (`cmd/crewlet/jsonout.go`) — a node's answer DECODED, every number kept as the digits the node sent, and printed again, because the wire escapes at its one writer and a copy of its bytes is exactly those escapes — and a walk refuses another encoder there — and so is a tier SCHEMA (`config.Schema`, the committed `schema/*.schema.json`), whose descriptions an editor shows on hover and a model authors against. A LEAF over the standard library, held to it by a test, because its readers sit at heights no other package spans — the tool layer imports the agent runtime, MCP and coordination; the ledger is held by the turn context, the prompt builder and the API and imports nothing from crewlet but this; a provider backend sits below the tool registry — and kept in the tool layer the rule had to be copied into the ledger, so two copies of it sat a package apart with nothing comparing them, and the provider backend would have needed a third. `coord/kv`'s activation encoder also writes unescaped and stays separate on purpose: its contract is a payload embedded byte-equal to `coord.CanonicalPayload`, a fact about a wire rather than about how text reads — and so does `sandbox/executor`'s `jsonStringify`, which spells a server's name as OpenCode's own `JSON.stringify` printed it, U+2028 and U+2029 literal, which `encoding/json` always escapes, to find it in OpenCode's answer. And JSON that did NOT decode is read as text too (`Unreadable`): every surface that decodes a caller's JSON — a body, a query's JSON parameter, a tool call's arguments on every transport (`toolargs.Decode`) — says which field held what where something else belongs, or where the document stops being JSON, and never encoding/json's own sentence, which names Go's types and struct fields; a second value after the first is `ErrTrailing`, said the same way |
| `toolname` | The engine's own TOOL NAMES that a package must SPELL without being able to import the tool's owner, declared once: the discovery pair (`list_mcp_server_tools`, `activate_tool`) the runner implements, which the skill guard exempts, the worker deny list keeps a child off, the ledger skips and the prompts and the delivery remedy tell a model to call — every one of them below the runner, and each had spelled the names again as a literal with nothing comparing the copies, so a rename compiled and left the guard exempting nothing, a worker inheriting its parent's `activate_tool` and every model told to call a tool that no longer existed — and every other name a package below its owner spells, on the same footing: the phase submissions (`submit_work`, `submit_review`) the skill guard exempts and the prompts and the round-cap judge's hint name, the onboarding pass's `reflect_and_persist` and `mark_onboarded`, the worker deny list's `a2a_ask`, `load_tool_skill`, `delegate` (the one name that is also an English word, so the walk reads it as the tool only where the text marks it as code), the tracker's ask pair (`create_work_item`, `comment_on_work_item`) the escalation guidance gates on and its `write_project` a caveat names, `lookup_colleague`, the `query_episodes` config's schema describes, a worker's `submit_result`, chat's `send_dm` and `search_messages`, and the artifacts' `create_artifact` and `list_dashboard_sources` a format's reference names; and `ask_and_wait`, the executor run's other ending beside `submit_work`, which every reader could import from the builtins but which no authority row names, so that, declared there alone, it was a name the walk never read; each owner's own constant and the authority table's row read the value here, so the walk covers the owner's copy too. A name EVERY reader can import stays with its owner; a name lands here only when some package that must spell it cannot import the one that owns it, or when it is a tool a phase surface offers of its own that no authority row names. A LEAF importing nothing, held to it by a test, because its readers include the prompts (which import only the org model and the provenance fence) and the skill guard (only the prompts, the logger, `words` and the tool layer's argument leaves, `tools/argval` and `tools/toolargs`). Its suite WALKS EVERY STRING LITERAL in the module's non-test Go (test-support packages, whose names end in `test`, excepted) for EVERY TOOL NAME A MODEL IS OFFERED, not only these — the authority table's tool-shaped verbs, which builtin's walks hold equal to the tools its registry and the operator catalogue register in both directions, and every name declared here, one set read in one place (`toolname/toolnametest`) that FAILS CLOSED, since the runner's suite holds every tool a phase surface offers beyond the seat's registry to it, so a tool neither source names fails there rather than dropping out of the walk unread (the tool bridge's `steer_take`, which a harness's hook calls and no surface lists, stays with its owner): a literal carrying one as a word of its own is either the name's ONE declaration (a constant's whole value — here, the tracker's, the chat's, the pages' or the builtins', or for every other artifacts tool the authority row builtin reads each name off) or a copy, which fails the build naming the file and line, and every name is declared exactly once. The table's own copies stand, held by those walks, and a word of ANOTHER VOCABULARY — an estate operation's wire name, an event payload's value, the inbox question's name, the Dashboard Assistant's own name for one of its tools, none of which a tool's rename may move — is a reviewed exemption whose stale entry fails, so a description, a schema, a refusal and a prompt build their text from the constant and a new copy is caught the day it is written. It used to read only this package's names, while the tools' own descriptions and refusals spelled some three hundred copies of names their owners declared |
| `toolname/toolnametest` | TEST-ONLY: the set of tool names `toolname`'s walk holds every string literal to (`Names`: the authority table's tool-shaped verbs and every constant `toolname` declares, read off its source), in ONE place, so the walk and every suite that holds a tool surface to the set (the runner's, over every tool a phase surface offers beyond the seat's registry) read the same set, and a tool a surface offers that the set lacks fails in that surface's suite rather than passing the walk unread. |
| `httpx` | The ONE outbound HTTP transport. Leaving `http.Client.Transport` nil is not "the defaults": it is `http.DefaultTransport`, whose `MaxIdleConnsPerHost` is **2, process-wide**, which seven vendor clients were silently sharing. One transport rather than one per client: a per-client clone is strictly worse than the default, since each keeps a private pool and reuses nothing between them. EVERY outbound client is built here, the operator CLI's included; a bare `&http.Client{}` outside this package is the bug, and a round tripper that wraps another takes `httpx.Transport()` as its base, which is the shape that slips through because it names no client at all. And the ONE PUBLIC-ADDRESS GUARD (ADR-0072): a URL a remote party named (a page, a server's metadata, a redirect) is sent only through `httpx.Guarded`, whose dialer judges the address the socket opens, never the name, so a resolver answering twice slips nothing past it; `httpx.Public` is the one rule of which addresses the public internet routes, an IPv4 address in IPv6 form or behind NAT64 judged as itself. A proxy the operator set is dialled as itself and only an address written into the URL is judged before it, since the proxy resolves a name on its own. An address the OPERATOR configured stays on `httpx.Client`, because a private one there is the deployment they chose |
| `modelcatalog` | What a model step SUGGESTS: each harness vendor's models, read from models.dev's public, keyless catalogue (`https://models.dev/api.json`, the one OpenCode reads). NOT A MODEL VENDOR and never handed a credential, so "no HTTP client to a model vendor" holds and no key leaves the engine to name models; whether a plan reaches a model it lists is the test call's answer. THIS NODE'S CACHE of a public document, in memory: fresh for 24 h (`Fresh`), then revalidated with `If-None-Match`; a failed fetch keeps the last copy and says how old it is, with no copy the answer is `source: none` and its reason, and a failure is not retried for 10 minutes (`RetryAfter`) so a blackholed egress costs one 30 s wait (`FetchBudget`) per window rather than one per page view. 16 MiB cap on a body of some 6 MB |
| `api/httpjson` | How every JSON surface answers. Four byte-identical `writeJSON` copies had already drifted into three spellings of one 413, and five sites handed `http.Error` a JSON literal, which sets `text/plain` **and** `nosniff`, the one combination that stops a strict client parsing it. EVERY CODE IS ON ITS TABLE WITH A SENTENCE, and every 503 goes through `Unavailable` / `UnavailableWith`, which write the `Retry-After` and the envelope together, ZERO meaning not retryable, because a node missing its keyring has none after any wait and a header saying "come back" teaches a client to hammer it. A state-log refusal gets its seconds from `statelog.RetryAfter` through `RetrySeconds` (rounded UP) on every surface, zero for one waiting cannot clear (an evicted node, a record this node cannot decode, a full log), the refusal's own derived hint where it has one, because two surfaces each turned those into `Retry-After: 2` and a client polled a node that would answer nothing until somebody readmitted or upgraded it. `/config`, `/secrets` and `/setup` had built their own `{"error": code}` bodies with no sentence, `/setup` had minted a dozen codes of its own (two of them second spellings of codes the table held), and a dozen 503s said "come back" without saying when; a source walk in `internal/api` now holds those surfaces, `/worklet` and `/webhooks` to all three rules, the webhook edge had answered `{"error": "invalid signature"}`, a code with a space in it, and a `{"status": "unavailable", "reason": …}` envelope of its own. EVERY 429 goes through `Throttled`, which carries the wait as a `Retry-After` ROUNDED UP and never below a second, the sign-in surface answered `throttled` with no header, so a client retried at once into the same refusal and a person read a lockout where there was a wait of seconds, and a walk over every file under `internal/api` refuses the status or the code spelled anywhere else. A STRICT BODY IS READ ONE WAY: `DeclaresJSON` (the media type a cross-site form cannot send without a preflight) and `DecodeStrict` (one value, no field the route does not declare, nothing after it) are what the act route and the assistant's routes both read a body with, where each had spelled both. A WRITE THAT MAY HAVE LANDED IS ONE ANSWER (`UnknownOutcome`), apart from a refusal that wrote nothing, because the two send a client opposite ways: `outcome: unknown`, the `op_id` to retry under where the route's writes carry one and NO KEY where they carry none (the config surface, whose answer names the revision it stored instead (an empty `op_id` is one a client would send back) and the node whose history holds it, since only that node can revert it), and no `Retry-After` on an unvouched one. `unauthorized`'s per-reason sentences hold EXACTLY the reasons `authz.RefusalFields` answers under that code, the completeness test deriving the set from the mapping, because a step-up, a machine token refused and an artifact not shared are answered under codes of their own and a row for one of them was a sentence no refusal could reach. And `Write` ESCAPES `<`, `>` and `&`, encoding/json's default, decided rather than left, as `stream.EncodeFrame` does for the socket: it protects nothing behind `nosniff` and a `default-src 'none'` policy and costs no decoder anything, but encoding/json re-escapes whatever a value embeds, so escaping at the one writer spells the whole wire alike, where switching it off there alone would spell it however each producer chose, which is why a reader that shows an answer as text (a command's `-json`, the dashboard) DECODES it and writes it again rather than showing the bytes as they came, and a test beside each writer holds the escaping. A STALE VERSION IS ANSWERED BY WHERE THE REQUEST STATED IT: named in an `If-Match` header and false it is `412` (RFC 9110 §13.1.1) — `version_moved`, or `revision_advanced` on `/config` and a forced `/setup` disconnect — and stated in a body or a tool argument it is the domain's `409`, which is what the dashboard's act route writes through; `configapi.RacedError.Status` is the config surface's statement of the rule (a lost activation race is `412` only where the request's If-Match named the revision, carried from the precondition check as `RacedError.Stated`, and `409` for no header, `*` or another resource's tag), and `TestEveryIfMatchReaderIsAReviewedPrecondition` holds every reader of the header under `internal/api` to it in both directions. EVERY ANSWER SAYS IT IS THE ENGINE'S (`EngineHeader`, `X-Crewlet-Engine: 1`), set once around everything a socket serves (`api.servedOn`), because a body cannot say whose answer it is: a proxy can answer JSON with an `error` field, and the dashboard requires the header before it reads an answer as the engine's (`dashboard/src/contract/engine.ts`, held both ways by a gate) |
| `api/opkey` | The HTTP half of an OPERATION ID: the one header a caller sends a key in (`Header`, `Idempotency-Key`), the rule a key it sends is held to (`Key`, the caller's, held to `statelog.CheckCallerOpID`, or one minted through `statelog.NewOpID`; refused `400 op_id_invalid` naming the header by `Refuse`), and the DIGEST of the request, its path, its query and its body re-encoded from the decoded value, that `/iam` steps a key by, so a key sent with ANOTHER request is another operation rather than the first one answered back from the ledger with nothing of the second written. Two write surfaces, the human write surface and the CLI each declared the header, the two each carried the digest byte for byte and all of them the key rule and its refusal, each commenting that it matched the others, which nothing compared, a retry is one script whichever surface it wrote to. What a write is published under beyond the binding stays each surface's, because what a write is ABOUT is the surface's vocabulary. A walk refuses the header's literal in any other non-test file. It also holds the ONE canonical encoding of a request (`Canonical`: path, sorted query and the decoded body re-encoded, each NUL-terminated), which `Digest` hashes to sixteen digits and `RequestDigest`/`RequestDigestOf` hash in full, under a domain of their own, for the Dashboard Assistant's approval and exec grant (ADR-0051): two bindings of a request to what it asked, one encoding, decoded one way on both sides |
| `api/paced` | How a streamed FILE BODY is bounded on the API listener: an upload read and a download written a mebibyte at a time, each inside the object store's one pace (`objstore.MiBPace`), and charged only for the time the client is waited on, never the store's. ONE PACKAGE for every surface that streams a file (a project's files in `api/workapi`, an artifact's uploads and downloads in `api/artifactsapi`), so no two surfaces hold one client to two paces. The server's own read and write timeouts are deliberately unset, which is why a route that streams has to bound itself |
| `api/pagepolicy` | What a BROWSER is told about every response: the Content-Security-Policy, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, and HSTS on https.
